  local minima in your search interval?
\end{enumerate}

\section{Constrained Optimization \& KKT
Conditions}\label{constrained-optimization-kkt-conditions}

\subsection{Theoretical Foundation}\label{theoretical-foundation-2}

\subsubsection{Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT)
Conditions}\label{karush-kuhn-tucker-kkt-conditions}

For constrained nonlinear optimization, KKT conditions provide necessary
conditions for optimality:

\textbf{Primal Problem:} \[
\begin{aligned}
\text{Minimize } & f(\mathbf{x}) \\
\text{Subject to } & g_i(\mathbf{x}) \leq 0, \quad i = 1, \ldots, m \\
& h_j(\mathbf{x}) = 0, \quad j = 1, \ldots, p
\end{aligned}
\]

\textbf{KKT Conditions:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Stationarity:}
  \(\nabla f(\mathbf{x}) + \sum \lambda_i \nabla g_i(\mathbf{x}) + \sum \mu_j \nabla h_j(\mathbf{x}) = 0\)
\item
  \textbf{Primal Feasibility:}
  \(g_i(\mathbf{x}) \leq 0, \quad h_j(\mathbf{x}) = 0\)
\item
  \textbf{Dual Feasibility:} \(\lambda_i \geq 0\)
\item
  \textbf{Complementary Slackness:} \(\lambda_i g_i(\mathbf{x}) = 0\)
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Interpretation}\label{interpretation}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Lagrange Multipliers (λ, μ):} Sensitivity of objective to
  constraint changes
\item
  \textbf{Complementary Slackness:} Inactive constraints have zero
  multipliers
\item
  \textbf{Stationarity:} Balance between objective improvement and
  constraint satisfaction
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Sample Problem}\label{sample-problem-4}

\textbf{Problem Statement:}

Minimize \(f(x,y) = x^2 + y^2\) subject to \(x + y \geq 4\).

\textbf{Solution:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Rewrite constraint:} \(g(x,y) = 4 - x - y \leq 0\)
\item
  \textbf{Lagrangian:}
  \(L(x,y,\lambda) = x^2 + y^2 + \lambda(4 - x - y)\)
\item
  \textbf{KKT Conditions:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\frac{\partial L}{\partial x} = 2x - \lambda = 0\)
  \item
    \(\frac{\partial L}{\partial y} = 2y - \lambda = 0\)
  \item
    \(\lambda(4 - x - y) = 0\)
  \item
    \(4 - x - y \leq 0\), \$\lambda \geq 0 \$
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Case 1:} λ = 0 → x=0, y=0 → violates constraint
\item
  \textbf{Case 2:} 4 - x - y = 0 → from stationarity: x=y=λ/2 → 4 - λ/2
  - λ/2 = 0 → λ=4
\item
  \textbf{Solution:} x=2, y=2, λ=4, f=8
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Sample Problem}\label{sample-problem-5}

\textbf{Problem Statement:} Campus City wants to minimize transportation
cost \(C(d) = 2d + 0.1d^2\) while ensuring at least 80\% of routes are
under 3km. Formulate as KKT problem.

\textbf{Solution:}

\textbf{Mathematical Formulation:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Objective: Minimize \(\sum 2d_i + 0.1d_i^2\)
\item
  Constraint: \(\frac{\text{count}(d_i \leq 3)}{n} \geq 0.8\)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{KKT Interpretation:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Lagrange multiplier indicates cost increase for relaxing the 80\%
  constraint
\item
  Helps decide if expanding the constraint is cost-effective
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Review Questions}\label{review-questions-4}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Explain the economic interpretation of Lagrange multipliers in
  constrained optimization.
\item
  What does complementary slackness tell us about active vs inactive
  constraints at the optimal solution?
\item
  Solve using KKT: Minimize \(f(x) = x^2\) subject to \(x \geq 2\).
\item
  How do KKT conditions extend the method of Lagrange multipliers for
  inequality constraints?
\item
  In a campus logistics context, what would a high Lagrange multiplier
  for an environmental constraint indicate?
\end{enumerate}

\section{Python Implementation of Nonlinear
Solvers}\label{python-implementation-of-nonlinear-solvers}

\subsection{Theoretical Foundation}\label{theoretical-foundation-3}

\subsubsection{Python Optimization
Ecosystem}\label{python-optimization-ecosystem}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{scipy.optimize}: Comprehensive optimization toolkit
\item
  \textbf{minimize() function}: Unified interface for multiple
  algorithms
\item
  \textbf{Algorithm choices}: SLSQP, trust-constr, COBYLA for
  constrained problems
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Key Functions}\label{key-functions}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ scipy.optimize }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ minimize}

\CommentTok{\# For unconstrained problems}
\NormalTok{result }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ minimize(fun, x0, method}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}BFGS\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# For constrained problems  }
\NormalTok{result }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ minimize(fun, x0, constraints}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{constraints, method}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}SLSQP\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Practical Considerations}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Initial guess (\(x_0\)): Critical for convergence
\item
  Gradient provision: Can significantly improve performance
\item
  Constraint formulation: Proper bounds and constraint objects
\end{itemize}

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection{Sample Problem}\label{sample-problem-6}

Problem Statement: Implement Golden Section search in \texttt{Python}
for \(f(x)=(x−4)^2+3\) on {[}0, 10{]}.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Solution:}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ math}

\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ golden\_section\_search(f, a, b, tol}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1e{-}6}\NormalTok{, max\_iter}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{    phi }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ math.sqrt(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{)) }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(max\_iter):}
\NormalTok{        x1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ a) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ phi}
\NormalTok{        x2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ a }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ (b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ a) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ phi}
        
        \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ f(x1) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ f(x2):}
\NormalTok{            a }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x1}
        \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{            b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x2}
            
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \BuiltInTok{abs}\NormalTok{(b }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ a) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ tol:}
            \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ (a }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b) }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ (a }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b) }\OperatorTok{/} \DecValTok{2}

\CommentTok{\# Test the function}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ objective(x):}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ (x }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{3}

\NormalTok{result }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ golden\_section\_search(objective, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Optimal x: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{result}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# Output: Optimal x: 4.0000}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
Optimal x: 4.0000
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Solution of constrained
NLPP}\label{solution-of-constrained-nlpp}

To solve NLPP using KKT method, we will use \texttt{solve()} function
from the \texttt{sympy} library. Key functions required for solution
will be imported

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ sympy }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ symbols, diff, solve}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Example 1:} Minimize \(f(x, y) = x^2 + y^2\) subject to
\(x + y \ge 4\) (Standard form: \(4 - x - y \le 0\)).
\end{quote}

\texttt{SymPy} does not inherently ``solve'' inequality constraints in
the same way a numerical solver (like \texttt{SciPy}) does. Instead, you
must manually transform the inequality into an equation using the
Complementary Slackness condition from the KKT framework.Here is the
logic: An inequality constraint \(g(x) \le 0\) is handled by introducing
a Lagrange multiplier \(\lambda\) and adding the equation
\(\lambda \cdot g(x) = 0\) to your system.

The 3-Step ``Filter'' WorkflowSince SymPy's solve function returns all
mathematical solutions (stationary points), you must add a ``Filter''
step to check the inequalities manually. - \emph{Formulate:} Convert
inequalities to KKT equations (Stationarity + Complementary Slackness).
- \emph{Solve:} Use \texttt{solve()} to find all candidate points. -
\emph{Filter:} Discard solutions that violate Primal Feasibility
(\(g(x) \le 0\)) or Dual Feasibility (\(\lambda \ge 0\)).

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python code:}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ sympy }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ symbols, diff, solve}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define Variables}
\NormalTok{x, y }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}x y\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{lam }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lambda\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Lagrange Multiplier}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Define Functions}
\NormalTok{f }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ y}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}           \CommentTok{\# Objective}
\NormalTok{g }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ y             }\CommentTok{\# Inequality constraint (g \textless{}= 0)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Formulate Lagrangian}
\CommentTok{\# L = f + lambda * g}
\NormalTok{L }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ lam }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Generate KKT Equations}
\CommentTok{\# A. Stationarity: Gradient of L must be zero}
\NormalTok{eq1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ diff(L, x)}
\NormalTok{eq2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ diff(L, y)}

\CommentTok{\# B. Complementary Slackness: lambda * constraint = 0}
\NormalTok{eq3 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Solve the System of Equations}
\CommentTok{\# We ask SymPy to find values for x, y, and lambda that satisfy these 3 equalities}
\NormalTok{solutions }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ solve([eq1, eq2, eq3], (x, y, lam), }\BuiltInTok{dict}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Candidate Solutions found: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(solutions)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Filter Solutions (The Crucial Step for Inequalities)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{{-}{-}{-} Validation Step {-}{-}{-}"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{valid\_solutions }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sol }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ solutions:}
\NormalTok{    x\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sol[x]}
\NormalTok{    y\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sol[y]}
\NormalTok{    lam\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sol[lam]}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Check Primal Feasibility: g(x) \textless{}= 0}
    \CommentTok{\# (Using a small tolerance for floating point comparisons if needed)}
\NormalTok{    primal\_check }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x\_val }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ y\_val) }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{0}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Check Dual Feasibility: lambda \textgreater{}= 0}
\NormalTok{    dual\_check }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam\_val }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}=} \DecValTok{0}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Testing Point (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{x\_val}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{y\_val}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) with lambda=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{lam\_val}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{:"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Constraint g \textless{}= 0? }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{primal\_check}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Dual lambda \textgreater{}= 0? }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{dual\_check}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ primal\_check }\KeywordTok{and}\NormalTok{ dual\_check:}
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{" VALID Optimal Solution"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        valid\_solutions.append(sol)}
    \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{" INVALID (Violates KKT conditions)"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Output Final Result}
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ valid\_solutions:}
\NormalTok{    opt }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ valid\_solutions[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Optimal Solution: x=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{opt[x]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, y=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{opt[y]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, cost=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{opt[x]}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ opt[y]}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
Candidate Solutions found: 2

--- Validation Step ---
Testing Point (0, 0) with lambda=0:
  Constraint g <= 0? False
  Dual lambda >= 0? True
 INVALID (Violates KKT conditions)
Testing Point (2, 2) with lambda=4:
  Constraint g <= 0? True
  Dual lambda >= 0? True
 VALID Optimal Solution

Optimal Solution: x=2, y=2, cost=8
\end{verbatim}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Example 2:} Solve \(f(x_1,x_2)=-x_1^2-x_2^2\);
\(-x_1-x_2\le -1\); \(-2x_1+2x_2\le 1\); \(x_1,x_2\ge 0\).
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ sympy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ sp}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define Variables}
\NormalTok{x1, x2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}x1 x2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{lam1, lam2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lambda1 lambda2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Define Objective (Maximization)}
\NormalTok{f }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{x1}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Define Constraints in Standard Form (g(x) \textless{}= 0)}
\CommentTok{\# Original: {-}x1 {-} x2 \textgreater{}= {-}1  {-}\textgreater{} x1 + x2 \textless{}= 1 {-}\textgreater{} x1 + x2 {-} 1 \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x1 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ x2 }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}

\CommentTok{\# Original: {-}2x1 + 2x2 \textless{}= 1 {-}\textgreater{} {-}2x1 + 2x2 {-} 1 \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g2 }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x1 }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x2 }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Formulate Lagrangian for Maximization (L = f {-} lambda*g)}
\NormalTok{L }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 5. KKT Equations}
\CommentTok{\# A. Stationarity (Gradient of L w.r.t x must be 0)}
\NormalTok{stat\_x1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, x1)}
\NormalTok{stat\_x2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, x2)}

\CommentTok{\# B. Complementary Slackness (lambda * g = 0)}
\NormalTok{slack\_1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1}
\NormalTok{slack\_2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Solve the System}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Solving KKT system..."}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# We assume non{-}negativity of x (x \textgreater{}= 0) is a boundary check rather than an explicit Lagrangian term }
\CommentTok{\# to keep the symbolic solver efficient for this demo.}
\NormalTok{solutions }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.solve([stat\_x1, stat\_x2, slack\_1, slack\_2], (x1, x2, lam1, lam2), }\BuiltInTok{dict}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 7. Validation \& Filtering}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Found }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(solutions)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ candidate points. Filtering for optimality..."}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{75}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Point (x1, x2)\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}20\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Multipliers\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}20\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Objective\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}10\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Status\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{75}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{best\_f }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}inf\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{best\_sol }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{None}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sol }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ solutions:}
    \CommentTok{\# Extract numerical values}
\NormalTok{    x1\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[x1])}
\NormalTok{    x2\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[x2])}
\NormalTok{    l1\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[lam1])}
\NormalTok{    l2\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[lam2])}
    
\NormalTok{    status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Valid"}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Check 1: Non{-}negativity of variables (x \textgreater{}= 0)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x1\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ x2\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (x \textless{} 0)"}
        
    \CommentTok{\# Check 2: Primal Feasibility (g \textless{}= 0)}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ (x1\_v }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ x2\_v }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (g1 \textgreater{} 0)"}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ (}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x1\_v }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x2\_v }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (g2 \textgreater{} 0)"}
        
    \CommentTok{\# Check 3: Dual Feasibility (lambda \textgreater{}= 0 for Max problem with L = f {-} lg)}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ l1\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ l2\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (lambda \textless{} 0)"}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Calculate Objective}
\NormalTok{    obj\_val }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{x1\_v}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2\_v}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{x1\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{x2\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{)   | L1=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{l1\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, L2=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{l2\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{obj\_val}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{     | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{status}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \StringTok{"Valid"} \KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ status }\KeywordTok{and}\NormalTok{ obj\_val }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}}\NormalTok{ best\_f:}
\NormalTok{        best\_f }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ obj\_val}
\NormalTok{        best\_sol }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (x1\_v, x2\_v)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{75}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ best\_sol:}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{ Optimal Solution: x1 = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{best\_sol[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{, x2 = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{best\_sol[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"   Max Objective Value = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{best\_f}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}

(0.5000, 0.5000)   | L1=-1.0000, L2=0.0000 | -0.5000     | Invalid (lambda < 0)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Example 3:} Minimize \(f(x_1,x_2)=2x_1+6x_2\) subject to
\(x_1-x_2\le 0\); \(x_1^2+x_2^2\ge 4\); \(x_1,x_2\ge 0\).
\end{quote}

Mathematical Formulation for \texttt{Sympy} requires constraints to be
in the form \(g(x) \ge 0\). We must rearrange the given inequalities: -
\emph{Objective:} Minimize \(f(x) = 2x_1 + 6x_2\) - \emph{Constraint 1:}
\(x_1 - x_2 \le 0 \implies x_2 - x_1 \ge 0\) - \emph{Constraint 2:}
\(x_1^2 + x_2^2 \ge 4 \implies x_1^2 + x_2^2 - 4 \ge 0\) -
\emph{Bounds:} \(x_1, x_2 \ge 0\)

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ sympy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ sp}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define Symbols}
\NormalTok{x1, x2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}x1 x2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{lam1, lam2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lambda1 lambda2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Define Objective (Minimization)}
\NormalTok{f }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x1 }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{6}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x2}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Define Constraints in Standard Form (g(x) \textless{}= 0)}
\CommentTok{\# Constraint 1: x1 {-} x2 \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ x1 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2}

\CommentTok{\# Constraint 2: x1\^{}2 + x2\^{}2 \textgreater{}= 4  {-}{-}\textgreater{} 4 {-} x1\^{}2 {-} x2\^{}2 \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g2 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x1}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Formulate Lagrangian (Minimization: L = f + sum(lambda * g))}
\NormalTok{L }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 5. KKT Equations}
\CommentTok{\# A. Stationarity (Gradient of L = 0)}
\NormalTok{eq\_x1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, x1)}
\NormalTok{eq\_x2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, x2)}

\CommentTok{\# B. Complementary Slackness (lambda * g = 0)}
\NormalTok{eq\_slack1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1}
\NormalTok{eq\_slack2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Solve the System}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Solving KKT system equations..."}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# This solves for stationary points and intersection points}
\NormalTok{solutions }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.solve([eq\_x1, eq\_x2, eq\_slack1, eq\_slack2], (x1, x2, lam1, lam2), }\BuiltInTok{dict}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 7. Filter \& Validate Solutions}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Found }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(solutions)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ candidate points. Validating KKT conditions..."}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{85}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Point (x1, x2)\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}20\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Multipliers\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}20\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Cost\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{:\textless{}10\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Status\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{85}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{optimal\_val }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}inf\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{optimal\_sol }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{None}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sol }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ solutions:}
    \CommentTok{\# Extract values (convert to float for comparison)}
\NormalTok{    x1\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[x1])}
\NormalTok{    x2\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[x2])}
\NormalTok{    l1\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[lam1])}
\NormalTok{    l2\_v }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol[lam2])}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Validation Logic}
\NormalTok{    status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Valid"}
    
    \CommentTok{\# 1. Domain Check (x \textgreater{}= 0)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ x1\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ x2\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (x \textless{} 0)"}
        
    \CommentTok{\# 2. Primal Feasibility (g \textless{}= 0)}
    \CommentTok{\# Check g1: x1 {-} x2 \textless{}= 0}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ (x1\_v }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2\_v) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (g1 \textgreater{} 0)"}
    \CommentTok{\# Check g2: 4 {-} x1\^{}2 {-} x2\^{}2 \textless{}= 0}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x1\_v}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ x2\_v}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (g2 \textgreater{} 0)"}
        
    \CommentTok{\# 3. Dual Feasibility (lambda \textgreater{}= 0 for Minimization)}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ l1\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ l2\_v }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}7}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (lambda \textless{} 0)"}
        
    \CommentTok{\# Calculate Cost}
\NormalTok{    cost }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x1\_v }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{6}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{x2\_v}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{x1\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{x2\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{)   | L1=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{l1\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, L2=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{l2\_v}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{ | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{cost}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{    | }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{status}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \StringTok{"Valid"} \KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ status }\KeywordTok{and}\NormalTok{ cost }\OperatorTok{\textless{}}\NormalTok{ optimal\_val:}
\NormalTok{        optimal\_val }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cost}
\NormalTok{        optimal\_sol }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (x1\_v, x2\_v)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{85}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ optimal\_sol:}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{ Optimal Solution: x1 = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{optimal\_sol[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, x2 = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{optimal\_sol[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"   Minimum Value = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{optimal\_val}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Insight for the user}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{Insight: The solution lies at the intersection of the line x1=x2 and the circle."}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
Solving KKT system equations...

Found 4 candidate points. Validating KKT conditions...
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Point (x1, x2)       | Multipliers          | Cost       | Status
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
(-0.6325, -1.8974)   | L1=0.0000, L2=-1.5811 | -12.6491    | Invalid (x < 0)
(0.6325, 1.8974)   | L1=0.0000, L2=1.5811 | 12.6491    | Valid
(-1.4142, -1.4142)   | L1=2.0000, L2=-1.4142 | -11.3137    | Invalid (x < 0)
(1.4142, 1.4142)   | L1=2.0000, L2=1.4142 | 11.3137    | Valid
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

 Optimal Solution: x1 = 1.4142, x2 = 1.4142
   Minimum Value = 11.3137

Insight: The solution lies at the intersection of the line x1=x2 and the circle.
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Support Vector Machine (SVM) Theory: A Constrained
Optimization
Perspective}\label{support-vector-machine-svm-theory-a-constrained-optimization-perspective}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Introduction \& Geometric Intuition}
\end{quote}

The Support Vector Machine (SVM) is a supervised learning algorithm that
finds the optimal hyperplane to separate two classes of data. Unlike
other linear classifiers, SVM seeks the \textbf{widest possible
separation} (margin) between the classes.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Hyperplane Equation}
\end{quote}

A hyperplane is defined by the equation: \[w^T x + b = 0\] - \(w\): The
weight vector (normal to the hyperplane). - \(b\): The bias term
(position relative to origin). - \(x\): The feature vector of a data
point.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Concept of the Margin}
\end{quote}

The ``Margin'' is the physical distance between the decision boundary
and the closest data points (Support Vectors).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Positive Boundary:} \(w^T x + b = +1\)
\item
  \textbf{Negative Boundary:} \(w^T x + b = -1\)
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Mathematical Derivation of the Margin}
\end{quote}

To calculate the width of the margin (\(M\)), consider two support
vectors, \(x_+\) on the positive boundary and \(x_-\) on the negative
boundary.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Equations:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(w^T x_+ + b = 1\)
  \item
    \(w^T x_- + b = -1\)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Difference:} Subtracting the second from the first:
  \[w^T (x_+ - x_-) = 2\]
\item
  \textbf{Projection:} The physical distance \(M\) is the projection of
  \((x_+ - x_-)\) onto the unit normal vector \(\frac{w}{||w||}\):
  \[M = (x_+ - x_-) \cdot \frac{w}{||w||} = \frac{w^T(x_+ - x_-)}{||w||} = \frac{2}{||w||}\]
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Key Insight:} To \textbf{maximize} the margin
(\(M = \frac{2}{||w||}\)), we must \textbf{minimize} the norm of the
weight vector (\(||w||\)).

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Constraint: Sign Agreement}
\end{quote}

For a classifier to be valid, it must correctly classify data points
\emph{outside} the margin. * If \(y_i = +1\), we require
\(w^T x_i + b \ge 1\). * If \(y_i = -1\), we require
\(w^T x_i + b \le -1\).

These two conditions can be compressed into a single inequality:
\[y_i (w^T x_i + b) \ge 1 \quad \forall i\]

This constraint ensures that every point is both correctly classified
and located safely outside the ``no-man's land'' of the margin.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Primal Optimization Problem}
\end{quote}

Minimizing \(||w||\) involves a square root, which is computationally
difficult. Instead, we minimize \(\frac{1}{2}||w||^2\), which is a
convex quadratic function with the same optimum.

\textbf{Standard Form (Hard Margin SVM):} \[
\begin{aligned}
& \text{Minimize} & & f(w, b) = \frac{1}{2}||w||^2 \\
& \text{Subject to} & & y_i(w^T x_i + b) \ge 1, \quad i = 1, \dots, N
\end{aligned}
\]

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Solving via Lagrange Multipliers (KKT Conditions)} To solve this
constrained problem, we construct the \emph{Lagrangian Function}:
\end{quote}

\[\mathcal{L}(w, b, \alpha) = \frac{1}{2}||w||^2 - \sum_{i=1}^{N} \alpha_i [y_i(w^T x_i + b) - 1]\]
where \(\alpha_i \ge 0\) are the Lagrange Multipliers.

The Karush-Kuhn-Tucker (KKT) Conditions

For optimality, the following conditions must hold:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Stationarity (\(\nabla \mathcal{L} = 0\)):}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\nabla_w \mathcal{L} = w - \sum \alpha_i y_i x_i = 0 \implies \mathbf{w = \sum \alpha_i y_i x_i}\)
  \item
    \(\nabla_b \mathcal{L} = -\sum \alpha_i y_i = 0 \implies \mathbf{\sum \alpha_i y_i = 0}\)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Primal Feasibility:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(y_i(w^T x_i + b) - 1 \ge 0\)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Dual Feasibility:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\alpha_i \ge 0\)
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Complementary Slackness:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\alpha_i [y_i(w^T x_i + b) - 1] = 0\)
  \item
    \emph{Interpretation:} If \(\alpha_i > 0\), the point lies exactly
    on the margin (\(y_i(w^T x + b) = 1\)). These points are the
    \textbf{Support Vectors}. If \(\alpha_i = 0\), the point is
    irrelevant to the boundary.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
Example Calculation
\end{quote}

\textbf{Data:} Class +1 at \((2,2)\), Class -1 at \((0,0)\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Lagrangian Formulation:}
  \[\mathcal{L} = \frac{1}{2}(w_1^2 + w_2^2) - \alpha_1 (2w_1 + 2w_2 + b - 1) - \alpha_2 (-b - 1)\]
\item
  \textbf{Solving:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    From stationarity: \(w_1 = 2\alpha, w_2 = 2\alpha\).
  \item
    From sum constraint: \(\alpha_1 = \alpha_2 = \alpha\).
  \item
    From active constraints: \(b = -1\), \(w_1 + w_2 = 1\).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Result:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\alpha = 0.25\)
  \item
    \textbf{Optimal Weights:} \(w = [0.5, 0.5]\)
  \item
    \textbf{Optimal Bias:} \(b = -1\)
  \item
    \textbf{Margin Width:} \(2/||w|| \approx 2.82\)
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python solution}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ sympy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ sp}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define Variables}
\NormalTok{w1, w2, b }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}w1 w2 b\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{lam1, lam2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.symbols(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lambda1 lambda2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, real}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Define Objective (Minimize 1/2 ||w||\^{}2)}
\NormalTok{f }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.5} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (w1}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ w2}\OperatorTok{**}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Define Constraints (g \textless{}= 0)}
\CommentTok{\# Constraint for Point A (2, 2): 1 {-} y(wx+b) \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g1 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{w1 }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{w2 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b))}
\CommentTok{\# Constraint for Point B (0, 0): 1 {-} y(wx+b) \textless{}= 0}
\NormalTok{g2 }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ (}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{w1 }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{0}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{w2 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ b))}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Formulate Lagrangian}
\NormalTok{L }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1 }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 5. KKT Conditions}
\CommentTok{\# A. Stationarity (Gradient of L = 0)}
\NormalTok{stat\_w1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, w1)}
\NormalTok{stat\_w2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, w2)}
\NormalTok{stat\_b  }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.diff(L, b)}

\CommentTok{\# B. Complementary Slackness (lambda * g = 0)}
\NormalTok{slack\_1 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam1 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g1}
\NormalTok{slack\_2 }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lam2 }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ g2}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Solve the System}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Solving KKT system..."}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# We explicitly ask for a dictionary result}
\NormalTok{solutions }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ sp.solve([stat\_w1, stat\_w2, stat\_b, slack\_1, slack\_2], }
\NormalTok{                     (w1, w2, b, lam1, lam2), }\BuiltInTok{dict}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 7. Validate Solutions}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Found }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(solutions)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ candidate solutions. Validating..."}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{)}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ sol }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ solutions:}
    \CommentTok{\# Use .get() to handle cases where a variable is free/undetermined}
    \CommentTok{\# If b is not in solution, it usually implies it\textquotesingle{}s arbitrary in that specific branch }
    \CommentTok{\# (e.g. if w=0, lambda=0, b might not be constrained by stationarity).}
    \CommentTok{\# We default to 0.0 for checking purposes or flag it.}
    \ControlFlowTok{try}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        val\_w1 }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol.get(w1, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{        val\_w2 }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol.get(w2, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{        val\_b  }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol.get(b, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)) }
\NormalTok{        val\_l1 }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol.get(lam1, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{        val\_l2 }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(sol.get(lam2, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}
    \ControlFlowTok{except} \PreprocessorTok{TypeError}\NormalTok{:}
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Skipping a symbolic/complex solution that couldn\textquotesingle{}t be converted to float."}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{continue}

\NormalTok{    status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Valid Optimal Solution"}

    \CommentTok{\# Check Primal Feasibility (g \textless{}= 0)}
    \CommentTok{\# Using a small tolerance (1e{-}6)}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{val\_w1 }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{2}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{val\_w2 }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ val\_b) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}6}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (Violates Point A Constraint)"}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ (}\DecValTok{1} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ val\_b) }\OperatorTok{\textgreater{}} \FloatTok{1e{-}6}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (Violates Point B Constraint)"}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Check Dual Feasibility (lambda \textgreater{}= 0)}
    \ControlFlowTok{elif}\NormalTok{ val\_l1 }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}6} \KeywordTok{or}\NormalTok{ val\_l2 }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \OperatorTok{{-}}\FloatTok{1e{-}6}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        status }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"Invalid (Negative Multiplier)"}

    \CommentTok{\# Print results}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Solution: w=(}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{val\_w1}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{val\_w2}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{), b=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{val\_b}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Multipliers: λ1=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{val\_l1}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{, λ2=}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{val\_l2}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Status: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{status}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
Solving KKT system...

Found 3 candidate solutions. Validating...
------------------------------------------------------------
Solution: w=(0.0000, 0.0000), b=0.0000
Multipliers: λ1=0.0000, λ2=0.0000
Status: Invalid (Violates Point A Constraint)
------------------------------------------------------------
Solution: w=(0.0000, 0.0000), b=-1.0000
Multipliers: λ1=0.0000, λ2=0.0000
Status: Invalid (Violates Point A Constraint)
------------------------------------------------------------
Solution: w=(0.5000, 0.5000), b=-1.0000
Multipliers: λ1=0.2500, λ2=0.2500
Status: Valid Optimal Solution
------------------------------------------------------------
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Principal Component Analysis
(PCA)}\label{principal-component-analysis-pca}

While often viewed as linear algebra, PCA is fundamentally a constrained
optimization problem seeking the direction of maximum variance.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Formulation}
\end{quote}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Objective:} Find a direction vector \(w\) that maximizes the
  variance of the projected data.
  \[\text{Maximize } f(w) = w^T \Sigma w\] \emph{(where \(\Sigma\) is
  the Covariance Matrix)}
\item
  \textbf{Constraint:} To prevent the vector length from growing
  infinitely to cheat the maximization, we fix its length to 1.
  \[\text{Subject to } g(w) = w^T w - 1 = 0\]
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Lagrangian Solution}
\end{quote}

We formulate the Lagrangian with multiplier \(\lambda\):
\[\mathcal{L}(w, \lambda) = w^T \Sigma w - \lambda(w^T w - 1)\]

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Finding the Optimum:} Differentiating with respect to \(w\)
  yields: \[2\Sigma w - 2\lambda w = 0 \implies \Sigma w = \lambda w\]
\end{itemize}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

The optimal direction \(w\) is simply the \textbf{Eigenvector} of the
covariance matrix, and the Lagrange multiplier \(\lambda\) is the
\textbf{Eigenvalue} (representing the amount of variance).

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection{Ridge Regression (L2
Regularization)}\label{ridge-regression-l2-regularization}

Ridge regression modifies standard least-squares fitting by imposing a
budget on the size of the coefficients to prevent overfitting.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Formulation}
\end{quote}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Objective:} Minimize the sum of squared prediction errors.
  \[\text{Minimize } ||y - X\beta||^2\]
\item
  \textbf{Constraint:} The total size (squared magnitude) of the
  coefficients must not exceed a budget \(t\).
  \[\text{Subject to } ||\beta||^2 \le t\]
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Lagrangian Solution}
\[\mathcal{L}(\beta, \lambda) = ||y - X\beta||^2 + \lambda(||\beta||^2 - t)\]
\end{quote}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Resulting Update Rule:} Solving for \(\nabla \mathcal{L} = 0\)
  gives the famous closed-form solution:
  \[\hat{\beta} = (X^T X + \lambda I)^{-1} X^T y\]
\end{itemize}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight:}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

The regularization parameter \(\lambda\) used in code is actually the
Lagrange Multiplier corresponding to the weight constraint.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection{Maximum Entropy Models
(MaxEnt)}\label{maximum-entropy-models-maxent}

Used in Natural Language Processing and ecology, \texttt{MaxEnt} seeks
the most ``unbiased'' probability distribution that still fits the
observed data.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Formulation}
\end{quote}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Objective:} Maximize the Entropy (uncertainty) of the
  distribution \(p(x)\).
  \[\text{Maximize } H(p) = -\sum p(x) \log p(x)\]
\item
  \textbf{Constraints:}

  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Normalization:} The probabilities must sum to 1.
    \[\sum p(x) = 1\]
  \item
    \textbf{Feature Matching:} The expected value of features in our
    model must match the empirical averages seen in the training data.
    \[\sum p(x) f_i(x) = \mathbb{E}_{data}[f_i]\]
  \end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{The Lagrangian Solution}
\end{quote}

Solving this Lagrangian system yields the \textbf{Gibbs (Softmax)
Distribution}:
\[p(y|x) = \frac{1}{Z} \exp\left(\sum \lambda_i f_i(x, y)\right)\]

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insights:}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

This derivation serves as the theoretical foundation for
\textbf{Logistic Regression} and the final layer of most \textbf{Neural
Networks}.

\end{tcolorbox}

\section{Review Questions}\label{review-questions-5}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  Explain the Golden Section Search algorithm.
\item
  Write the key steps in the Golden Section Search algorithm with a
  suitable example.
\item
  What are the KKT conditions and how do they relate to Lagrange
  multipliers?
\item
  Find an approximate solution for the unconstrained objective function,
  \(f(x)=(x-3)^2+2\) in the domain \([0,5]\) using the Golden Section
  Search algorithm in 4 steps.
\item
  Solve the non-linear optimization problem: Minimize
  \(f(x_1,x_2 )=x_1^2+x_2^2\) Subject to the constraints:
  \(x_1+x_2\ge 4; x_1,x_2\ge 0\).
\item
  A medical device company wants to determine the optimal coating
  thickness (between 0 mm and 5 mm) that minimizes production cost. The
  cost function is experimentally modeled as \(F(x)=(x-3)^2+2\). Using
  the Golden Section Search method, perform 4 iterations to approximate
  the optimal thickness that minimizes cost.
\item
  A machine learning engineer wants to optimize two model parameters
  x\_1 and x\_2 to minimize the regularized loss function:
  \(f(x,y)=x_1^2-4x_1+x_2^2-6x_2\) with two constraint -- complexity
  constraint and non-negativity weight restrictions given by
  \(x_1+x_2\le 3; x_1,x_2\ge 0\), respectively. Formulate an appropriate
  mathematical programming model to represent this problem and determine
  the optimal parameter values.
\item
  What does complementary slackness tell us about active vs inactive
  constraints at the optimal solution? Explain your claims using an
  appropriate example.
\item
  What does complementary slackness tell us about active vs inactive
  constraints at the optimal solution? Explain your claims using an
  appropriate example.
\item
  Find an interval of solution for \(f(x)=100/x+2x\) in \([10,200]\) in
  4 steps using Golden Section or Fibonacci search method.
\item
  Solve the following nonlinear optimization problem using
  Karush--Kuhn--Tucker conditions: Minimize:
  \(f(x,y)=(x_1-1)^2+(x_2-2)^2\) subject to :
  \(x_1+x_2-5\ge 0;x_1,x_2\ge 0\).
\end{enumerate}

\chapter{Module 3: Project Planning and Optimization
Techniques}\label{module-3-project-planning-and-optimization-techniques}

\section{Module Overview}\label{module-overview-2}

\subsection{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Master project planning methodologies: CPM and PERT
\item
  Implement network analysis for project scheduling
\item
  Create and interpret Gantt charts for project visualization
\item
  Apply heuristic algorithms for optimization problems
\item
  Develop practical scheduling solutions for campus projects
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Module Structure}\label{module-structure-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.1}: Introduction to Project Planning
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.2}: Critical Path Method (CPM)
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.3}: Program Evaluation and Review Technique (PERT)
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.4}: Gantt Charts and Project Visualization
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.5}: Heuristic Algorithms and Local Search
\item
  \textbf{Section 3.6}: Micro-Project 3: Campus Construction Planning
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Real-World Context}\label{real-world-context-1}

In Module 1 and 2, we looked at how to optimize a system when we know
the variables and the constraints. But in the real world---especially in
engineering and software development---\textbf{time} is the most
precious constraint of all.

Have you ever wondered how massive projects, like building our new
campus library or launching a software product like ChatGPT, actually
finish on time? It isn't magic; it's mathematics.

In this module, we are going to learn the art and science of
\textbf{Scheduling}. We will move from ``I hope we finish on time'' to
``I calculate we have a 95\% probability of finishing by Friday.'' Apply
project planning techniques to campus construction projects, facility
maintenance schedules, and resource allocation for campus events.

\section{Introduction to Project
Planning}\label{introduction-to-project-planning}

\subsection{The Engineering of Time}\label{the-engineering-of-time}

Project planning is essentially an optimization problem where we try to
minimize time (\(T\)) or Cost (\(C\)) while respecting a complex web of
dependencies.

Imagine you are cooking a meal. You cannot fry the onions before you
chop them. That is a \textbf{dependency}. However, you \emph{can} boil
the water while you chop the onions. That is \textbf{parallelism}.
Project planning is simply the mathematical modeling of these
dependencies and parallel tasks.

\subsection{Theoretical Foundation}\label{theoretical-foundation-4}

We represent a project as a \textbf{Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)},
denoted as \(G = (V, E)\).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Vertices (\(V\)):} These are events or milestones (e.g.,
  ``Foundation poured'').
\item
  \textbf{Edges (\(E\)):} These are the activities that take time (e.g.,
  ``Pouring Concrete - 3 days'').
\item
  \textbf{Weights:} The duration or cost attached to an edge.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Mathematical
Representation}\label{mathematical-representation}

A project can be represented as a directed graph \(G = (V, E)\) where:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Vertices (V)}: Represent project milestones or activity
  completion points
\item
  \textbf{Edges (E)}: Represent activities with associated durations
\item
  \textbf{Weights}: Activity durations or costs
\end{itemize}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

Why ``Acyclic''? Because if you have a cycle (A depends on B, B depends
on A), you have an infinite loop. In project management, that means the
project never finishes! We must avoid cycles.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection{Sample Scenario: The Library
Construction}\label{sample-scenario-the-library-construction}

Let's look at a concrete example. We are building a library. Here is the
logic:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Activity & Description & Predecessors & Duration (weeks) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
A & Site Prep & - & 2 \\
B & Foundation & A & 4 \\
C & Framing & B & 6 \\
D & Electrical & C & 3 \\
E & Plumbing & C & 3 \\
F & Interior & D, E & 4 \\
G & Exterior & C & 2 \\
H & Inspection & F, G & 1 \\
\end{longtable}

Do you see activity \textbf{F}? It cannot start until \textbf{both} D
and E are finished. This is where the math gets interesting.

\subsubsection{What is Project
Planning?}\label{what-is-project-planning}

Project planning involves defining project objectives, determining tasks
and resources needed, and establishing timelines for project completion.
In optimization context, it's about finding the most efficient sequence
of activities to minimize time or cost.

\subsubsection{Key Components of Project
Planning}\label{key-components-of-project-planning}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Activities/Tasks}: Discrete work elements with defined
  durations
\item
  \textbf{Dependencies}: Precedence relationships between activities
\item
  \textbf{Resources}: Personnel, equipment, and materials required
\item
  \textbf{Time Estimates}: Duration for each activity
\item
  \textbf{Milestones}: Significant checkpoints in project timeline
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Project Management
Objectives}\label{project-management-objectives}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Time Minimization}: Complete project in shortest possible time
\item
  \textbf{Cost Optimization}: Minimize total project cost
\item
  \textbf{Resource Leveling}: Smooth resource utilization over time
\item
  \textbf{Risk Mitigation}: Identify and manage critical activities
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Campus City Applications}\label{campus-city-applications}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Construction of new campus facilities
\item
  Maintenance scheduling for existing buildings
\item
  Event planning and resource allocation
\item
  Academic calendar optimization
\item
  Emergency response planning
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Sample Problem}\label{sample-problem-7}

\textbf{Problem Statement:} Campus City is planning a new library
construction with the following activities:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Activity & Description & Immediate Predecessors & Duration (weeks) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
A & Site preparation & - & 2 \\
B & Foundation work & A & 4 \\
C & Structural framing & B & 6 \\
D & Electrical work & C & 3 \\
E & Plumbing work & C & 3 \\
F & Interior work & D, E & 4 \\
G & Exterior finishing & C & 2 \\
H & Final inspection & F, G & 1 \\
\end{longtable}

Construct the project network and identify the project completion time.

\textbf{Solution Approach:} We'll represent this as a network diagram
with nodes as activity completion points and edges as activities. The
solution will be developed in Section 3.2.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  What are the key differences between project planning and continuous
  optimization problems?
\item
  Explain how project dependencies create sequencing constraints that
  must be respected in scheduling.

  Describe three campus scenarios where project planning techniques
  would provide significant benefits over ad-hoc scheduling.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Critical Path Method (CPM)}\label{critical-path-method-cpm}

\subsection{The ``Bottleneck'' Concept}\label{the-bottleneck-concept}

The Critical Path Method (CPM) is one of the most powerful tools in an
engineer's toolkit. Here is the intuition: \textbf{A project is only as
fast as its slowest sequence of dependent tasks.}

We call this longest sequence the \textbf{Critical Path}. If any
activity on this path is delayed by one day, the \emph{entire project}
is delayed by one day.

\subsubsection{What is Critical Path
Method?}\label{what-is-critical-path-method}

CPM is an algorithm for scheduling project activities that:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Identifies critical activities that cannot be delayed without delaying
  the project
\item
  Calculates the minimum project duration
\item
  Determines float/slack time for non-critical activities
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{CPM Terminology}\label{cpm-terminology}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Earliest Start Time (ES)}: Earliest time an activity can begin
\item
  \textbf{Earliest Finish Time (EF)}: ES + Activity Duration
\item
  \textbf{Latest Start Time (LS)}: Latest time an activity can begin
  without delaying project
\item
  \textbf{Latest Finish Time (LF)}: LS + Activity Duration
\item
  \textbf{Float/Slack}: LF - EF or LS - ES (flexibility in scheduling)
\item
  \textbf{Critical Path}: Sequence of activities with zero float
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{CPM Algorithm Steps}\label{cpm-algorithm-steps}

\textbf{Forward Pass (Calculate ES, EF):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Start with initial activity: ES = 0, EF = Duration
\item
  For each subsequent activity: ES = max(EF of all predecessors)
\item
  EF = ES + Duration
\item
  Project duration = max(EF of all terminal activities)
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Backward Pass (Calculate LS, LF):}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  For terminal activities: LF = Project duration, LS = LF - Duration
\item
  For each preceding activity: LF = min(LS of all successors)
\item
  LS = LF - Duration
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Identify Critical Path:} Activities where ES = LS (or EF = LF)
have zero float and are on critical path.

\subsubsection{Mathematical
Formulation}\label{mathematical-formulation-2}

For activity \(i\) with duration \(d_i\):

\[
\begin{aligned}
ES_i &= \max_{j \in P(i)} EF_j \\
EF_i &= ES_i + d_i \\
LF_i &= \min_{j \in S(i)} LS_j \\
LS_i &= LF_i - d_i \\
\text{Float}_i &= LS_i - ES_i = LF_i - EF_i
\end{aligned}
\]

Where \(P(i)\) are predecessors and \(S(i)\) are successors.

\section{Practice Problems: Critical Path Method
(CPM)}\label{practice-problems-critical-path-method-cpm}

\subsection{The Systematic Approach: The ``Pass''
Method}\label{the-systematic-approach-the-pass-method}

To solve CPM problems without getting confused, we treat the project
network like a circuit. We don't guess; we calculate.

\textbf{The 4-Step Algorithm:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Forward Pass (The Earliest Possible):}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Start at Time 0.
  \item
    Move \(Start \to End\).
  \item
    \textbf{Rule:} \(ES = \max(EF \text{ of predecessors})\).
  \item
    \emph{Logic:} You can't start until \emph{all} your prerequisites
    are done.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Backward Pass (The Latest Permissible):}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Start at the Project End Time.
  \item
    Move \(End \to Start\).
  \item
    \textbf{Rule:} \(LF = \min(LS \text{ of successors})\).
  \item
    \emph{Logic:} You must finish early enough so \emph{everyone}
    waiting for you can start on time.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Calculate Float (The Buffer):}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \(\text{Float} = LS - ES\) (or \(LF - EF\)).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Identify Critical Path:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Any activity with \textbf{Zero Float} is Critical.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Problem 1: The ``Hello World'' of
CPM}\label{problem-1-the-hello-world-of-cpm}

\textbf{Scenario:} A simple linear workflow with one parallel branch.

\textbf{Activity Data:}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Activity & Predecessors & Duration (Days) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
A & - & 3 \\
B & A & 4 \\
C & A & 2 \\
D & B, C & 5 \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

\textbf{Step 1: Forward Pass (Find ES, EF)}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{A:} Start at 0. Duration 3. \(\to EF = 3\).
\item
  \textbf{B:} Predecessor A (ends at 3). Start at 3. Duration 4.
  \(\to EF = 7\).
\item
  \textbf{C:} Predecessor A (ends at 3). Start at 3. Duration 2.
  \(\to EF = 5\).
\item
  \textbf{D:} Predecessors B (ends 7) and C (ends 5).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \emph{Rule:} We must wait for the \textbf{slowest} one.
    \(\max(7, 5) = 7\).
  \item
    Start at 7. Duration 5. \(\to EF = 12\).
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Project Duration:} 12 Days.

\textbf{Step 2: Backward Pass (Find LF, LS)}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{D:} End at 12. Duration 5. \(\to LS = 12 - 5 = 7\).
\item
  \textbf{B:} Successor D (starts at 7). Must finish by 7. Duration 4.
  \(\to LS = 7 - 4 = 3\).
\item
  \textbf{C:} Successor D (starts at 7). Must finish by 7. Duration 2.
  \(\to LS = 7 - 2 = 5\).
\item
  \textbf{A:} Successors B (starts 3) and C (starts 5).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \emph{Rule:} Must be done in time for the \textbf{earliest}
    requirement. \(\min(3, 5) = 3\).
  \item
    Must finish by 3. Duration 3. \(\to LS = 0\).
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Step 3 \& 4: Float \& Critical Path}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}llllllll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Activity & Duration & ES & EF & LS & LF & Float (LS-ES) & Critical? \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
A & 3 & 0 & 3 & 0 & 3 & 0 & \textbf{Yes} \\
B & 4 & 3 & 7 & 3 & 7 & 0 & \textbf{Yes} \\
C & 2 & 3 & 5 & 5 & 7 & 2 & No \\
D & 5 & 7 & 12 & 7 & 12 & 0 & \textbf{Yes} \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Critical Path:} \(A \to B \to D\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python solution}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ pandas }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ pd}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define the Project Data}
\NormalTok{tasks }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: \{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}predecessors\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: []\},}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: \{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}predecessors\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]\},}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: \{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}predecessors\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]\},}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: \{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}predecessors\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]\}}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Build the Network Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.DiGraph()}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ task, data }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks.items():}
\NormalTok{    G.add\_node(task, duration}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{data[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{])}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ pred }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ data[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}predecessors\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]:}
\NormalTok{        G.add\_edge(pred, task)}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# 3. Forward Pass (Calculate Early Start \& Early Finish)}
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# We iterate in topological order to ensure predecessors are processed first}
\NormalTok{early\_start }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}
\NormalTok{early\_finish }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ task }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ nx.topological\_sort(G):}
    \CommentTok{\# ES is max of predecessor\textquotesingle{}s EF. If no predecessors, ES = 0}
\NormalTok{    predecessors }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(G.predecessors(task))}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ predecessors:}
\NormalTok{        es }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}
    \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        es }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(early\_finish[p] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ p }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ predecessors)}
    
\NormalTok{    duration }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ G.nodes[task][}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    ef }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ es }\OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ duration}
    
\NormalTok{    early\_start[task] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ es}
\NormalTok{    early\_finish[task] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ef}

\NormalTok{project\_duration }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(early\_finish.values())}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# 4. Backward Pass (Calculate Late Start \& Late Finish)}
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# We iterate in reverse topological order}
\NormalTok{late\_start }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}
\NormalTok{late\_finish }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ task }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{reversed}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(nx.topological\_sort(G))):}
\NormalTok{    successors }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(G.successors(task))}
    
    \CommentTok{\# LF is min of successor\textquotesingle{}s LS. If no successors, LF = Project Duration}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ successors:}
\NormalTok{        lf }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ project\_duration}
    \ControlFlowTok{else}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        lf }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(late\_start[s] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ s }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ successors)}
        
\NormalTok{    duration }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ G.nodes[task][}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    ls }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lf }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ duration}
    
\NormalTok{    late\_finish[task] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ lf}
\NormalTok{    late\_start[task] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ ls}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# 5. Calculate Slack \& Identify Critical Path}
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\NormalTok{slack }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{\}}
\NormalTok{critical\_path }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}

\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ task }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks:}
\NormalTok{    s }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ late\_start[task] }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ early\_start[task]}
\NormalTok{    slack[task] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ s}
    \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ s }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{        critical\_path.append(task)}

\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# 6. Display Results}
\CommentTok{\# {-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}}
\CommentTok{\# Create a DataFrame for a clean CPM Table}
\NormalTok{df }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ pd.DataFrame(\{}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [tasks[t][}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}duration\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}ES (Early Start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [early\_start[t] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}EF (Early Finish)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [early\_finish[t] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}LS (Late Start)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [late\_start[t] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}LF (Late Finish)\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [late\_finish[t] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Slack\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [slack[t] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ t }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ tasks],}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Critical?\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Yes\textquotesingle{}} \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ s }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0} \ControlFlowTok{else} \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}No\textquotesingle{}} \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ s }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ slack.values()]}
\NormalTok{\}, index}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{tasks.keys())}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}{-}{-} CPM Schedule Table {-}{-}{-}"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(df)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"} \OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{30}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Project Duration: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{project\_duration}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ Days"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Critical Path: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{} {-}\textgreater{} \textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{join(critical\_path)}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Visualize the Network}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spring\_layout(G)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw(G, pos, with\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2000}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}skyblue\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
--- CPM Schedule Table ---
   Duration  ES (Early Start)  EF (Early Finish)  LS (Late Start)  \
A         3                 0                  3                0   
B         4                 3                  7                3   
C         2                 3                  5                5   
D         5                 7                 12                7   

   LF (Late Finish)  Slack Critical?  
A                 3      0       Yes  
B                 7      0       Yes  
C                 7      2        No  
D                12      0       Yes  
------------------------------
Total Project Duration: 12 Days
Critical Path: A -> B -> D
\end{verbatim}
