  between linear programming and combinatorial optimization?}
\end{enumerate}

\section{Graph Algorithms I - Minimum Spanning
Trees}\label{graph-algorithms-i---minimum-spanning-trees}

Welcome to the world of Graphs. If you learn only one data structure in
your entire engineering career, let it be the Graph. It is the universal
language of connections. \#\#\# Theoretical Foundation

To an engineer, the world is not a list of isolated items; it is a web
of relationships. We model these relationships using a mathematical
structure called a \emph{Graph}.

Formally, a graph \(G = (V, E)\) consists of two fundamental sets:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Vertices (\(V\)):} These are the ``Entities'' or the ``Nodes''
  in our network.

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    In a \textbf{Logistics} context, \(V\) represents warehouses,
    customers, or intersections.
  \item
    In a \textbf{Social Network}, \(V\) represents people.
  \item
    In a \textbf{Computer Network}, \(V\) represents servers or routers.
  \item
    \emph{Notation:} The size of this set is often denoted as \(|V|\) or
    \(n\).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Edges (\(E\)):} These are the ``Connections'' or ``Links''
  between the vertices. An edge connects two vertices \(u\) and \(v\).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    If the connection is \textbf{Weighted}, we associate a value
    \(w_{uv}\) with the edge.
  \item
    This weight represents the ``cost'' of the relationship: distance
    (km), latency (ms), or construction cost (\$).
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Why do weights matter?}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

In unweighted graphs, we only care \emph{if} a connection exists. In
weighted graphs---which is what we deal with in engineering
optimization---we care about \emph{how expensive} that connection is.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsubsection{Minimum Spanning Tree
(MST)}\label{minimum-spanning-tree-mst}

Now, imagine you are a Civil Engineer for Campus City. You need to
connect 10 new buildings to the electrical grid. You can dig trenches
between any of them, but digging is expensive.

You have three requirements:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Reach Everyone:} Every building must have power.
\item
  \textbf{No Redundancy:} You don't want loops. If Building A connects
  to B, and B connects to C, adding a cable from A to C is a waste of
  money (A is already powered via B).
\item
  \textbf{Minimize Cost:} You want the total length of all cables to be
  as low as possible.
\end{enumerate}

This problem is exactly what a \textbf{Minimum Spanning Tree (MST)}
solves. Let's break down the terminology:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{``Spanning'':} It spans the graph, meaning it includes
  \emph{all} vertices in \(V\). No node is left isolated.
\item
  \textbf{``Tree'':} In graph theory, a ``tree'' is a connected graph
  with \textbf{no cycles} (loops).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \emph{Key Property:} If a graph has \(n\) vertices, a spanning tree
    will have exactly \(n-1\) edges. Any more edges, and you create a
    cycle; any fewer, and the graph becomes disconnected.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{``Minimum'':} Among all possible trees that connect these
  nodes, we want the one where the sum of edge weights is the smallest.
\end{itemize}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Mathematical Definition:}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

For a connected, undirected, weighted graph \(G=(V, E)\), an MST is a
subgraph \(T \subseteq E\) such that: \[
\text{Minimize } W(T) = \sum_{(u,v) \in T} w_{uv}
\] Subject to the constraint that \(T\) connects all vertices and
contains no cycles.

A spanning tree connects all vertices of a graph without cycles,
minimizing total edge weight.

\end{tcolorbox}

\textbf{Applications in Campus City:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Designing campus utility networks
\item
  Planning transportation routes
\item
  Creating efficient communication networks
\item
  Optimizing facility connections
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Other Real-World Applications:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Telecommunications:} Laying fiber optic cables to connect
  cities.
\item
  \textbf{Cluster Analysis:} In AI, MSTs are used to find natural
  groupings in data.
\item
  \textbf{Image Segmentation:} Grouping pixels to identify objects in
  computer vision.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Prim's Algorithm- The Strategy of
``Growth''}\label{prims-algorithm--the-strategy-of-growth}

Prim's Algorithm is like a spreading infection or a growing mold. We
start from a single point---a ``seed''---and expand outwards, always
grabbing the nearest node to our existing cluster.

It is a quintessential Greedy Algorithm. At every single step, we make
the locally optimal choice (the cheapest edge available right now), and
because of the mathematical properties of Spanning Trees, this
guarantees a globally optimal result.

\textbf{Algorithm Steps:}

To implement this, imagine dividing your graph vertices into two sets:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Visited Set (\(S\)): Nodes already connected to our tree.
\item
  The Unvisited Set (\(V-S\)): Nodes we haven't reached yet.
\end{itemize}

Step-by-step logic:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \emph{Initialization:} Start with an arbitrary vertex (it doesn't
  matter which one, let's say Node A). This is the ``root'' of our tree.
  The Visited Set contains only \(\{A\}\).
\item
  \emph{Identify the Frontier:} Look at all the edges connecting a node
  inside our Visited Set to a node in the Unvisited Set. We call these
  the ``crossing edges.
\item
  \emph{``The Greedy Selection:} From these crossing edges, select the
  one with the minimum weight. Let's say this edge connects Node A to
  Node B.
\item
  \emph{Expansion:} Add Node B to our Visited Set. Add the edge
  \((A, B)\) to our MST.
\item
  \emph{Repeat:} Now, look at all edges from both A and B to the
  remaining unvisited nodes. Pick the cheapest one again. Repeat this
  process until the Visited Set contains all vertices.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Complexity:} \(O(E \log V)\) with binary heap

\section{Practice Problems: Prim's
Algorithm}\label{practice-problems-prims-algorithm}

To truly master Prim's Algorithm, you cannot just read about it. You
must trace the execution by hand. This helps you debug code later
because you understand the state changes of the set \(S\) (Visited) and
\(V-S\) (Unvisited).

Here are three problems ranging from basic mechanics to matrix
interpretation.

\subsubsection{Problem 1:}\label{problem-1-1}

\textbf{Objective:} Find the Minimum Spanning Tree (MST) and its total
cost for the following weighted graph.

\textbf{Graph Data:} * \textbf{Vertices:} \(\{A, B, C, D, E\}\) *
\textbf{Edges \& Weights:} * \((A, B) = 4\) * \((A, C) = 2\) *
\((B, C) = 3\) * \((B, D) = 2\) * \((B, E) = 3\) * \((C, D) = 4\) *
\((C, E) = 5\) * \((D, E) = 1\)

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

We will start with \textbf{Vertex A} as our root. * \textbf{\(S\)
(Visited Set):} The set of nodes in our tree. * \textbf{Frontier:} The
candidate edges connecting \(S\) to the rest of the graph.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 10\tabcolsep) * \real{0.1667}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Step
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Current Set \(S\)
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Candidate Edges (From \(S\) to Outside)
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Decision (Min Weight)
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
New Vertex Added
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Cost
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{Start} & \(\{A\}\) & \((A,B)=4, (A,C)=2\) & Pick \((A,C)\) &
\(C\) & 2 \\
\textbf{2} & \(\{A, C\}\) & \((A,B)=4, (C,B)=3, (C,D)=4, (C,E)=5\) &
Pick \((C,B)\) & \(B\) & 3 \\
\textbf{3} & \(\{A, C, B\}\) & \((A,B)\) is now internal.Frontier:
\((B,D)=2, (B,E)=3, (C,D)=4, (C,E)=5\) & Pick \((B,D)\) & \(D\) & 2 \\
\textbf{4} & \(\{A, C, B, D\}\) & Frontier:
\((B,E)=3, (C,E)=5, (D,E)=1\) & Pick \((D,E)\) & \(E\) & 1 \\
\textbf{End} & \(\{A, B, C, D, E\}\) & All vertices included. & Stop. &
- & - \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Final MST Edges:} \(\{(A,C), (C,B), (B,D), (D,E)\}\)
\textbf{Total Minimum Cost:} \(2 + 3 + 2 + 1 = \mathbf{8}\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python implementation}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx }\CommentTok{\# for network visualization}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Create the Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Add Edges and Weights (from Problem 1 Data)}
\CommentTok{\# Format: (Node1, Node2, Weight)}
\NormalTok{edges }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}E\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}E\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}E\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{G.add\_weighted\_edges\_from(edges)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Compute Minimum Spanning Tree using Prim\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm}
\CommentTok{\# Note: networkx\textquotesingle{}s minimum\_spanning\_tree uses Kruskal\textquotesingle{}s by default, }
\CommentTok{\# but for undirected graphs the result is identical in weight.}
\CommentTok{\# We can explicitly ask for Prim\textquotesingle{}s if needed, though the outcome is the MST.}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}prim\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Calculate Total Cost}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Minimum Spanning Tree Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Edges in MST: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{(mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{))}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spring\_layout(G, seed}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# Seed for consistent layout}

\CommentTok{\# Draw all edges (thin, gray)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# Draw MST edges (thick, red)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}red\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{2.5}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{700}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}skyblue\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# Draw Labels (Node Names)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# Draw Edge Weights}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.get\_edge\_attributes(G, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Prim\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm Result (Total Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Minimum Spanning Tree Results ===
Edges in MST: [('A', 'C', {'weight': 2}), ('B', 'D', {'weight': 2}), ('B', 'C', {'weight': 3}), ('D', 'E', {'weight': 1})]
Total Minimum Cost: 8.0
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-2-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{Problem 2:}\label{problem-2}

\textbf{Objective:} In computer memory, graphs are often stored as
matrices. Find the MST for the graph represented by this Adjacency
Matrix. (Note: \(\infty\) or \(0\) implies no direct connection).

\[
\begin{matrix}
 & \mathbf{1} & \mathbf{2} & \mathbf{3} & \mathbf{4} & \mathbf{5} \\
\mathbf{1} & 0 & 2 & 0 & 6 & 0 \\
\mathbf{2} & 2 & 0 & 3 & 8 & 5 \\
\mathbf{3} & 0 & 3 & 0 & 0 & 7 \\
\mathbf{4} & 6 & 8 & 0 & 0 & 9 \\
\mathbf{5} & 0 & 5 & 7 & 9 & 0
\end{matrix}
\]

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

Let's start from \textbf{Vertex 1}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Initialize:} \(S = \{1\}\).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{Available choices from Row 1:} Node 2 (weight 2), Node 4
    (weight 6).
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Select edge \((1, 2)\). Cost = 2.
  \item
    \textbf{\(S = \{1, 2\}\)}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Update Frontier:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Edges from 1: \((1, 4) = 6\).
  \item
    Edges from 2: \((2, 3) = 3\), \((2, 4) = 8\), \((2, 5) = 5\).
  \item
    \textbf{Min Edge:} \((2, 3)\) with weight 3.
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Select \((2, 3)\). Cost = 3.
  \item
    \textbf{\(S = \{1, 2, 3\}\)}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Update Frontier:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Edges from 1: \((1, 4) = 6\).
  \item
    Edges from 2: \((2, 4) = 8\), \((2, 5) = 5\).
  \item
    Edges from 3: \((3, 5) = 7\).
  \item
    \textbf{Min Edge:} \((2, 5)\) with weight 5.
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Select \((2, 5)\). Cost = 5.
  \item
    \textbf{\(S = \{1, 2, 3, 5\}\)}
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Update Frontier:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Edges from 1: \((1, 4) = 6\).
  \item
    Edges from 2: \((2, 4) = 8\).
  \item
    Edges from 3: \((3, 5)\) is now internal.
  \item
    Edges from 5: \((5, 4) = 9\).
  \item
    \textbf{Min Edge:} \((1, 4)\) with weight 6.
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Select \((1, 4)\). Cost = 6.
  \item
    \textbf{\(S = \{1, 2, 3, 5, 4\}\)}
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Total Minimum Cost:} \(2 + 3 + 5 + 6 = \mathbf{16}\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python solution}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define the Adjacency Matrix}
\CommentTok{\# In this representation:}
\CommentTok{\# {-} Values \textgreater{} 0 represent edge weights.}
\CommentTok{\# {-} Values == 0 represent NO connection (except the diagonal).}
\NormalTok{adj\_matrix }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.array([}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# Node 1}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# Node 2}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# Node 3}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# Node 4}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]   }\CommentTok{\# Node 5}
\NormalTok{])}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Convert Matrix to Graph}
\CommentTok{\# NetworkX has a handy function for this. }
\CommentTok{\# It creates a graph where indices (0,1,2...) become nodes.}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.from\_numpy\_array(adj\_matrix)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Relabel Nodes (Optional but good for consistency)}
\CommentTok{\# The matrix indices are 0{-}4, but our problem used 1{-}5.}
\NormalTok{mapping }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{: }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{\}}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.relabel\_nodes(G, mapping)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Compute MST using Prim\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}prim\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Output the Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Matrix MST Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges in the MST:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, data }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) with weight }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{data[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spring\_layout(G, seed}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Position nodes clearly}

\CommentTok{\# Draw the underlying graph (faintly) to show all possibilities}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#e0e0e0\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw the MST (bold)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}green\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{2.5}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes and Labels}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{800}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#90EE90\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Light green}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Edge Labels (Weights) for the MST only (to keep it clean)}
\NormalTok{mst\_edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{(u, v): d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)\}}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst\_edge\_labels)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\StringTok{"MST from Adjacency Matrix"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Matrix MST Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 16.0
Edges in the MST:
  Edge (1 - 2) with weight 2
  Edge (1 - 4) with weight 6
  Edge (2 - 3) with weight 3
  Edge (2 - 5) with weight 5
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-3-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{Problem 3:}\label{problem-3}

\textbf{Objective:} Sometimes, two edges have the same weight. Does it
matter which one we pick? Use Prim's algorithm on the following graph to
find out.

\textbf{Graph Data:} * \textbf{Vertices:} \(\{P, Q, R, S\}\) *
\textbf{Edges:} * \((P, Q) = 5\) * \((P, R) = 5\) * \((Q, R) = 10\) *
\((Q, S) = 5\) * \((R, S) = 5\)

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

Let's start with \textbf{Vertex P}.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Step 1:} \(S = \{P\}\).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Choices: \((P, Q)=5\), \((P, R)=5\).
  \item
    \textbf{Observation:} It is a tie. In Prim's algorithm, \textbf{it
    does not matter} which one you pick; the total cost will be the
    same.
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Let's arbitrarily pick \textbf{\((P, Q)\)}. Cost =
    5.
  \item
    \(S = \{P, Q\}\).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Step 2:} \(S = \{P, Q\}\).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Choices from P: \((P, R)=5\).
  \item
    Choices from Q: \((Q, R)=10\), \((Q, S)=5\).
  \item
    \textbf{Tie again:} Between \((P, R)\) and \((Q, S)\).
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Let's pick \textbf{\((Q, S)\)}. Cost = 5.
  \item
    \(S = \{P, Q, S\}\).
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Step 3:} \(S = \{P, Q, S\}\).

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Choices from P: \((P, R)=5\).
  \item
    Choices from Q: \((Q, R)=10\).
  \item
    Choices from S: \((S, R)=5\).
  \item
    \textbf{Tie again:} Between \((P, R)\) and \((S, R)\). Both connect
    to the remaining node R.
  \item
    \textbf{Action:} Let's pick \textbf{\((P, R)\)}. Cost = 5.
  \item
    \(S = \{P, Q, S, R\}\).
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Total Minimum Cost:} \(5 + 5 + 5 = \mathbf{15}\).

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python solution}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Create the Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Add Edges (The "Diamond" with equal weights)}
\CommentTok{\# Notice we have multiple edges with weight 5}
\NormalTok{edges }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}P\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Q\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}P\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}R\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Q\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}R\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Q\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}S\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}R\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}S\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{G.add\_weighted\_edges\_from(edges)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Compute MST}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}prim\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Output Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Tie{-}Breaker MST Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges selected by the algorithm:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) Weight: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# Custom layout to make the "Diamond" shape clear}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}P\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{), }
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Q\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{), }
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}R\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{), }
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}S\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{\# Draw background edges (dashed)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, alpha}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw MST edges (solid, thick purple)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}purple\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#DDA0DD\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Plum color}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Weights}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.get\_edge\_attributes(G, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"One Possible MST (Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Hide axis for cleaner look}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Tie-Breaker MST Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 15.0
Edges selected by the algorithm:
  Edge (P - Q) Weight: 5
  Edge (P - R) Weight: 5
  Edge (Q - S) Weight: 5
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-4-output-2.pdf}}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

Notice that if we had chosen differently in Step 3 (picking \(S-R\)
instead of \(P-R\)), the tree structure would look different, but the
\textbf{Total Cost} would still be 15. The MST structure is not always
unique, but the MST \emph{Cost} is always unique.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsubsection{Kruskal's Algorithm- Mergers and
Acquisitions}\label{kruskals-algorithm--mergers-and-acquisitions}

While Prim's algorithm behaves like a growing mold spreading from a
single center, \textbf{Kruskal's Algorithm} takes a global view. It
doesn't care about ``connectivity'' initially. It simply looks at the
entire list of possible connections (edges) and says: \emph{``I want the
cheapest one}.

Imagine a map of an archipelago with 100 islands.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Prim's} would pick one island and build a bridge to its
  nearest neighbor, then the next nearest, slowly connecting the chain.
\item
  \textbf{Kruskal's} builds bridges all over the map simultaneously. It
  connects Island A to B (because it's cheap), and Island Y to Z
  (because it's cheap). Eventually, these small clusters (called a
  ``Forest'' of trees) merge into larger clusters, until suddenly,
  everything is connected.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{ALgorithm}

Kruskal's relies on a data structure logic called \textbf{Union-Find}
(or Disjoint Set) to keep track of which nodes are already connected.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Sort the Edges:} List every single edge in the graph and sort
  them by weight from \textbf{Smallest to Largest}.
\item
  \textbf{Iterate and Select:} Start picking edges from the top of your
  sorted list (the cheapest ones).
\item
  \textbf{The Cycle Check:} For each edge \((u, v)\), ask: \emph{``Are
  \(u\) and \(v\) already in the same connected component?''}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \textbf{No (Safe):} Add the edge to your MST. These two separate
    components are now ``United.''
  \item
    \textbf{Yes (Cycle Hazard):} If they are already connected (perhaps
    via a long, winding path elsewhere), adding this edge would create a
    cycle (a loop). \textbf{Discard} this edge immediately.
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Termination:} Stop when you have added \(V-1\) edges (where
  \(V\) is the number of vertices).
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Complexity:} \$ O(E \log E) \$ for sorting

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-tip-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-tip-color}{\faLightbulb}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight: When to use which?}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-tip-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

Since Kruskal's depends on sorting \emph{edges}, it is generally
preferred for \textbf{Sparse Graphs} (where there are few edges,
\(E \approx V\)). If the graph is \textbf{Dense} (lots of edges,
\(E \approx V^2\)), the sorting takes too long, and Prim's is usually
faster.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsubsection{Comparison}\label{comparison}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Aspect & Prim's Algorithm & Kruskal's Algorithm \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Approach & Vertex-based growth & Edge-based selection \\
Data Structure & Priority queue & Union-Find (Disjoint Set) \\
Best for & Dense graphs & Sparse graphs \\
Implementation & Slightly more complex & Simpler to implement \\
\end{longtable}

\subsection{Practice Problems: Kruskal's
Algorithm}\label{practice-problems-kruskals-algorithm}

Kruskal's Algorithm is conceptually different from Prim's because it
focuses on the \textbf{Edges}, not the Nodes. The secret to solving
these problems by hand is discipline: \textbf{List, Sort, and Check.}

Here are four problems to master this technique.

Solve the campus building connection problem from Section 4.1 using
Kruskal's algorithm.

\textbf{Solution Steps:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{List all edges sorted by weight:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    AE: 200
  \item
    AB: 300\\
  \item
    CD: 300
  \item
    BC: 350
  \item
    DE: 350
  \item
    AC: 400
  \item
    CE: 400
  \item
    BD: 450
  \item
    AD: 500
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Build MST using Kruskal's:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Add AE (200): Tree = \{A, E\}
  \item
    Add AB (300): Tree = \{A, B, E\}
  \item
    Add CD (300): Tree = \{A, B, E\}, \{C, D\} (two components)
  \item
    Add BC (350): Connects components → Tree = \{A, B, C, D, E\}
  \item
    Total edges = 4 (V-1 = 4), so stop
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{MST Edges:} AE (200), AB (300), CD (300), BC (350)
\item
  \textbf{Total Length:} 200 + 300 + 300 + 350 = 1150 meters
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Problem 1: \textbf{Objective:} Find the
MST cost for the following
graph.}{Problem 1: Objective: Find the MST cost for the following graph.}}\label{problem-1-objective-find-the-mst-cost-for-the-following-graph.}

\textbf{Graph Data:} * \textbf{Vertices:} \(\{A, B, C, D\}\) *
\textbf{Edges:} * \((A, B) = 10\) * \((A, C) = 6\) * \((A, D) = 5\) *
\((B, D) = 15\) * \((C, D) = 4\)

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

\textbf{Step 1: The Sorting Phase} List all edges and sort them by
weight (Low \(\to\) High).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \((C, D) = 4\)
\item
  \((A, D) = 5\)
\item
  \((A, C) = 6\)
\item
  \((A, B) = 10\)
\item
  \((B, D) = 15\)
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Step 2: The Selection Phase} We iterate through the sorted list.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Edge
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Weight
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Action
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Reasoning
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\((C, D)\) & 4 & \textbf{Select} & \(C\) and \(D\) are distinct. \\
\((A, D)\) & 5 & \textbf{Select} & \(A\) is not connected to
\(\{C, D\}\) yet. \\
\((A, C)\) & 6 & \textbf{Reject} & Cycle Alert! \(A\) connects to \(D\),
and \(D\) connects to \(C\). Path \(A-D-C\) exists. \\
\((A, B)\) & 10 & \textbf{Select} & \(B\) is isolated. Connect it to
\(A\). \\
\((B, D)\) & 15 & \textbf{Reject} & \(B\) and \(D\) are already
connected via \(A\). \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Final MST:} \(\{(C,D), (A,D), (A,B)\}\) \textbf{Total Cost:}
\(4 + 5 + 10 = \mathbf{19}\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python code}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Initialize the Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Add Edges (From Problem 1 Data)}
\CommentTok{\# Format: (Node1, Node2, Weight)}
\NormalTok{edges }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{G.add\_weighted\_edges\_from(edges)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Compute MST using Kruskal\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm}
\CommentTok{\# NetworkX allows us to specify the algorithm.}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}kruskal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Calculate Total Cost}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Kruskal\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges in the MST:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) Weight: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spring\_layout(G, seed}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# Consistent layout}

\CommentTok{\# Draw the Background Graph (All edges, dashed gray)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels\_all }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.get\_edge\_attributes(G, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels\_all, font\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw the MST (Selected edges, solid red)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}red\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{2.5}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes and Labels}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{800}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#FFD700\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Gold color}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Kruskal\textquotesingle{}s Algorithm (Total Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Kruskal's Algorithm Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 19.0
Edges in the MST:
  Edge (A - D) Weight: 5
  Edge (A - B) Weight: 10
  Edge (C - D) Weight: 4
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-5-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Problem 2: \textbf{Objective:} Observe
how Kruskal's builds separate disconnected trees (``Forests'') before
merging
them.}{Problem 2: Objective: Observe how Kruskal's builds separate disconnected trees (``Forests'') before merging them.}}\label{problem-2-objective-observe-how-kruskals-builds-separate-disconnected-trees-forests-before-merging-them.}

\textbf{Graph Data:} * \textbf{Vertices:} \(\{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6\}\) *
\textbf{Edges:} * \((1, 2) = 2\) * \((3, 4) = 2\) * \((5, 6) = 2\) *
\((2, 3) = 10\) * \((4, 5) = 10\)

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

\textbf{Step 1: Sorted Edges} 1. \((1, 2) = 2\) 2. \((3, 4) = 2\) 3.
\((5, 6) = 2\) 4. \((2, 3) = 10\) 5. \((4, 5) = 10\)

\textbf{Step 2: Selection Phase}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Edge
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Weight
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Action
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Resulting Sets (Components)
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\((1, 2)\) & 2 & \textbf{Select} &
\(\{1,2\}, \{3\}, \{4\}, \{5\}, \{6\}\) \\
\((3, 4)\) & 2 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{1,2\}, \{3,4\}, \{5\}, \{6\}\) \\
\((5, 6)\) & 2 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{1,2\}, \{3,4\}, \{5,6\}\) \\
\((2, 3)\) & 10 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{1,2,3,4\}, \{5,6\}\) (Merges two
clusters) \\
\((4, 5)\) & 10 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{1,2,3,4,5,6\}\) (Merges final
cluster) \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Total Cost:} \(2+2+2+10+10 = \mathbf{26}\)

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, colback=white, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, arc=.35mm, opacityback=0, toptitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, bottomtitle=1mm, titlerule=0mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Insight}, toprule=.15mm, left=2mm, coltitle=black, breakable, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, opacitybacktitle=0.6]

Notice that for the first three steps, we created three separate,
isolated islands. Only at the very end did the ``heavy'' bridges connect
them. Prim's algorithm would not have done this; it would have been
forced to cross a heavy bridge earlier to keep growing.

\end{tcolorbox}

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python Code:}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Initialize Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Add Edges (Problem 2 Data)}
\CommentTok{\# Note: The logic of the problem implies a chain structure: 1{-}2...3{-}4...5{-}6}
\NormalTok{edges }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{G.add\_weighted\_edges\_from(edges)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Compute MST}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}kruskal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Output Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Forest Merger Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges in the MST:"}\NormalTok{)}
\CommentTok{\# We sort the edges by weight in the print statement to clearly show }
\CommentTok{\# the order Kruskal likely selected them (Islands first, then Bridges).}
\NormalTok{sorted\_mst\_edges }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{sorted}\NormalTok{(mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{), key}\OperatorTok{=}\KeywordTok{lambda}\NormalTok{ x: x[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{][}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{])}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ sorted\_mst\_edges:}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) Weight: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{)) }\CommentTok{\# Wide aspect ratio for the chain}

\CommentTok{\# Use a spectral layout to spread the chain out horizontally}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spectral\_layout(G)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Background Edges}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw MST Edges (Blue)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}blue\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{2.5}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{600}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightcoral\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, font\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}white\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Weights}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.get\_edge\_attributes(G, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Visualizing the Chain (Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Forest Merger Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 26.0
Edges in the MST:
  Edge (1 - 2) Weight: 2
  Edge (3 - 4) Weight: 2
  Edge (5 - 6) Weight: 2
  Edge (2 - 3) Weight: 10
  Edge (4 - 5) Weight: 10
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-6-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Problem 3: \textbf{Objective:} Identify
exactly when a cycle is formed and
rejected.}{Problem 3: Objective: Identify exactly when a cycle is formed and rejected.}}\label{problem-3-objective-identify-exactly-when-a-cycle-is-formed-and-rejected.}

\textbf{Graph Data:}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Vertices:} \(\{X, Y, Z\}\)
\item
  \textbf{Edges:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    \((X, Y) = 5\)
  \item
    \((Y, Z) = 5\)
  \item
    \((X, Z) = 50\) (The heavy link)
  \end{itemize}
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

\textbf{Step 1: Sorted Edges}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \((X, Y) = 5\)
\item
  \((Y, Z) = 5\)
\item
  \((X, Z) = 50\)
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Step 2: Selection Phase}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Edge
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Weight
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Action
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Reasoning
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\((X, Y)\) & 5 & \textbf{Select} & Connects \(X\) to \(Y\). \\
\((Y, Z)\) & 5 & \textbf{Select} & Connects \(Z\) to the group
\(\{X, Y\}\). \\
\((X, Z)\) & 50 & \textbf{Reject} & \(X\) is already connected to \(Z\)
via \(Y\) (\(X \to Y \to Z\)). Adding this would close the triangle. \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Total Cost:} \(5 + 5 = \mathbf{10}\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python code}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Initialize Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Add Edges (Problem 3 Data)}
\CommentTok{\# A simple triangle: X{-}Y (5), Y{-}Z (5), X{-}Z (50)}
\NormalTok{edges }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}X\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Y\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Y\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Z\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}X\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Z\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# The expensive link that creates a cycle}
\NormalTok{]}

\NormalTok{G.add\_weighted\_edges\_from(edges)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Compute MST}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}kruskal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Output Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Cycle Trap Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges in the MST:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) Weight: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Check if the expensive edge was included}
\ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not}\NormalTok{ mst.has\_edge(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}X\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Z\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{[Algorithm Check]: The edge (X{-}Z) with weight 50 was REJECTED as expected."}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}

\CommentTok{\# Custom position to form a nice triangle}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}X\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Y\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{1.5}\NormalTok{),}
    \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Z\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{: (}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\CommentTok{\# Draw ALL edges (including the rejected one) as dashed lines}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.5}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, alpha}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw only MST edges as solid thick lines}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}green\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgreen\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Edge Labels}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.get\_edge\_attributes(G, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Cycle Rejection Visualized (Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Cycle Trap Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 10.0
Edges in the MST:
  Edge (X - Y) Weight: 5
  Edge (Y - Z) Weight: 5

[Algorithm Check]: The edge (X-Z) with weight 50 was REJECTED as expected.
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-7-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{\texorpdfstring{Problem 4: \textbf{Objective:} In data
science, you often get a Distance Matrix. Extract the
MST.}{Problem 4: Objective: In data science, you often get a Distance Matrix. Extract the MST.}}\label{problem-4-objective-in-data-science-you-often-get-a-distance-matrix.-extract-the-mst.}

\textbf{Distance Matrix:}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lllll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
& P & Q & R & S \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\textbf{P} & 0 & 4 & 9 & 0 \\
\textbf{Q} & 4 & 0 & 2 & 8 \\
\textbf{R} & 9 & 2 & 0 & 7 \\
\textbf{S} & 0 & 8 & 7 & 0 \\
\end{longtable}

\emph{(Note: 0 indicates no direct connection, except on diagonal)}

\textbf{Mathematical Solution:}

\textbf{Step 1: Extract and Sort Edges} First, read the upper triangle
of the matrix to get the unique edges: * \((P, Q) = 4\) * \((P, R) = 9\)
* \((Q, R) = 2\) * \((Q, S) = 8\) * \((R, S) = 7\)

\textbf{Sorted List:} 1. \((Q, R) = 2\) 2. \((P, Q) = 4\) 3.
\((R, S) = 7\) 4. \((Q, S) = 8\) 5. \((P, R) = 9\)

\textbf{Step 2: Selection Phase}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Edge
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Weight
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Action
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Connected Components
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\((Q, R)\) & 2 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{Q, R\}\) \\
\((P, Q)\) & 4 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{P, Q, R\}\) \\
\((R, S)\) & 7 & \textbf{Select} & \(\{P, Q, R, S\}\) \\
\((Q, S)\) & 8 & \textbf{Reject} & Cycle (\(Q\) connects to \(R\), \(R\)
connects to \(S\)) \\
\((P, R)\) & 9 & \textbf{Reject} & Cycle (\(P\) connects to \(Q\), \(Q\)
connects to \(R\)) \\
\end{longtable}

\textbf{Total Cost:} \(2 + 4 + 7 = \mathbf{13}\)

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python code:}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Define the Distance Matrix}
\CommentTok{\# 0 represents no direct connection (except diagonal)}
\CommentTok{\# Rows/Cols correspond to P, Q, R, S}
\NormalTok{dist\_matrix }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.array([}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# P}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# Q}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{],  }\CommentTok{\# R}
\NormalTok{    [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]   }\CommentTok{\# S}
\NormalTok{])}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Convert Matrix to Graph}
\CommentTok{\# This creates nodes 0, 1, 2, 3 automatically}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.from\_numpy\_array(dist\_matrix)}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Relabel Nodes to P, Q, R, S}
\CommentTok{\# This makes the output human{-}readable}
\NormalTok{mapping }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{: }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}P\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{: }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Q\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{: }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}R\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{: }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}S\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{\}}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.relabel\_nodes(G, mapping)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Compute MST using Kruskal\textquotesingle{}s}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}kruskal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Output Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"=== Matrix{-}based MST Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Edges selected:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Edge (}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ {-} }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{) Weight: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.circular\_layout(G)  }\CommentTok{\# Circular layout works well for matrix data}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Background Graph (dashed)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw MST (solid orange)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}orange\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1000}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#FFE4B5\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Moccasin color}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Weights (only for MST to keep it readable)}
\NormalTok{mst\_edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{(u, v): d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{] }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)\}}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst\_edge\_labels, font\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}darkorange\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f"MST from Distance Matrix (Cost: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{verbatim}
=== Matrix-based MST Results ===
Total Minimum Cost: 13.0
Edges selected:
  Edge (P - Q) Weight: 4
  Edge (Q - R) Weight: 2
  Edge (R - S) Weight: 7
\end{verbatim}

\pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{Module-4_files/figure-pdf/cell-8-output-2.pdf}}

\subsubsection{Problem 5}\label{problem-5}

Campus City wants to create a pedestrian pathway network connecting 6
key locations with minimum construction cost. The distances (in meters)
and construction costs (\$ per meter) are:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Connection & Distance & Cost/meter \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
A-B & 400 & \$50 \\
A-C & 300 & \$60 \\
A-D & 500 & \$45 \\
B-C & 350 & \$55 \\
B-E & 450 & \$50 \\
C-D & 400 & \$60 \\
C-F & 300 & \$65 \\
D-F & 350 & \$55 \\
E-F & 400 & \$50 \\
\end{longtable}

Find the minimum spanning tree for construction cost.

\textbf{Solution:}

First calculate total cost for each edge:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  AB: 400 × 50 = \$20,000
\item
  AC: 300 × 60 = \$18,000
\item
  AD: 500 × 45 = \$22,500
\item
  BC: 350 × 55 = \$19,250
\item
  BE: 450 × 50 = \$22,500
\item
  CD: 400 × 60 = \$24,000
\item
  CF: 300 × 65 = \$19,500
\item
  DF: 350 × 55 = \$19,250
\item
  EF: 400 × 50 = \$20,000
\end{itemize}

Apply Prim's or Kruskal's to find MST with minimum total cost.

\begin{quote}
\textbf{Python code:}
\end{quote}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ networkx }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ nx}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}

\CommentTok{\# 1. Setup the Graph}
\NormalTok{G }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.Graph()}

\CommentTok{\# 2. Input Data}
\CommentTok{\# Format: (Node1, Node2, Distance\_Meters, Cost\_Per\_Meter)}
\NormalTok{raw\_data }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{400}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}A\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{500}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{45}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{350}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{55}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}B\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}E\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{450}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{400}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}C\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}F\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{300}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{65}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}D\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}F\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{350}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{55}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{    (}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}E\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}F\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{400}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{50}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{]}

\CommentTok{\# 3. Transform Data and Build Graph}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}{-}{-} Calculating Edge Weights {-}{-}{-}"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, dist, rate }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ raw\_data:}
    \CommentTok{\# The Optimization Metric is TOTAL COST, not just distance}
\NormalTok{    total\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ dist }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ rate}
    
    \CommentTok{\# We store \textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{} for the algorithm, but keep \textquotesingle{}dist\textquotesingle{} and \textquotesingle{}rate\textquotesingle{} for reference}
\NormalTok{    G.add\_edge(u, v, weight}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{total\_cost, distance}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{dist, rate}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{rate)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Edge }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{{-}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{dist}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{m * $}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{rate}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{/m = $}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{:,\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 4. Compute MST (Minimizing Total Cost)}
\NormalTok{mst }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.minimum\_spanning\_tree(G, algorithm}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}kruskal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{total\_project\_cost }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mst.size(weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 5. Output Results}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{=== Optimization Results ==="}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Total Minimum Construction Cost: $}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{total\_project\_cost}\SpecialCharTok{:,.2f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Selected Pathways:"}\NormalTok{)}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u, v, d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ mst.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{):}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"  Connect }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{u}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{{-}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{v}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ (Cost: $}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\SpecialCharTok{:,\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# 6. Visualization}
\NormalTok{plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{7}\NormalTok{))}
\NormalTok{pos }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ nx.spring\_layout(G, seed}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{42}\NormalTok{)  }\CommentTok{\# Consistent layout}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Background Graph (dashed)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}lightgray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, style}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dashed\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw MST (Solid Green)}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edges(G, pos, edgelist}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mst.edges(), edge\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}green\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, width}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Draw Nodes}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_nodes(G, pos, node\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{800}\NormalTok{, node\_color}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}\#98FB98\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\CommentTok{\# Pale Green}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_labels(G, pos, font\_weight}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}bold\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Label Edges with their Calculated COST (since that\textquotesingle{}s what we optimized)}
\CommentTok{\# Dividing by 1000 to show "k" (e.g., 20k) to keep the chart clean}
\NormalTok{edge\_labels }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{(u,v): }\SpecialStringTok{f"$}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{d[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}weight\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}\OperatorTok{/}\DecValTok{1000}\SpecialCharTok{:.1f\}}\SpecialStringTok{k"} \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ u,v,d }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ G.edges(data}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)\}}
\NormalTok{nx.draw\_networkx\_edge\_labels(G, pos, edge\_labels}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{edge\_labels, font\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{plt.title(}\StringTok{"Pedestrian Pathway MST (Based on Construction Cost)"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{plt.show()}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

  \textbf{Explain why a minimum spanning tree has exactly V-1 edges for
  a graph with V vertices.}
\item
  \textbf{Compare Prim's and Kruskal's algorithms in terms of when each
  would be preferred for campus network design.}
\item
  \textbf{Given a complete graph with 8 vertices, how many possible
  spanning trees exist? How does this illustrate combinatorial
  explosion?}
\item
