\textbf{Statistical conclusion:} The model achieves high accuracy on the
test set, with a classification report showing strong precision, recall,
and F1-score across all classes. The confusion matrix indicates that the
model is effectively distinguishing between the three iris species, with
minimal misclassifications. The training and validation curves suggest
good convergence without significant overfitting, demonstrating the
effectiveness of the optimization techniques applied in this module.

\textbf{Major problems with the implementation:} The model may require
tuning of hyperparameters such as learning rate, batch size, and the
number of epochs to achieve optimal performance. Additionally, the use
of dropout layers may lead to underfitting if not properly balanced with
the model architecture and training data. Finally, the small dataset
size may limit the generalizability of the model, and further validation
on larger datasets would be necessary to confirm its robustness.

\textbf{Statistical issues with this loss function:} The use of
categorical cross-entropy loss is appropriate for multi-class
classification problems like the iris dataset. However, if the dataset
is imbalanced, this loss function may not adequately penalize
misclassifications of minority classes, leading to biased predictions.
In such cases, alternative loss functions or techniques such as class
weighting or oversampling may be necessary to address class imbalance
and improve model performance.

\textbf{Issues with optimization of average loss:} The optimization of
average loss may not always lead to the best generalization performance,
especially if the dataset contains outliers or is imbalanced. In such
cases, the model may overfit to the majority class or be influenced by
outliers, resulting in suboptimal performance on unseen data. To
mitigate this issue, techniques such as robust loss functions,
regularization, or data augmentation can be employed to improve the
model's ability to generalize and perform well on a wider range of
inputs.

\textbf{Truth of pixel-wise error minimization:} Pixel-wise error
minimization may not always lead to the best perceptual quality in
image-related tasks, as it focuses on minimizing the average error
across all pixels rather than considering the overall structure and
context of the image. This can result in blurry or unrealistic outputs,
especially in tasks such as image generation or super-resolution. To
address this issue, alternative loss functions that consider perceptual
quality, such as adversarial loss or perceptual loss, can be used to
improve the visual quality of the generated images. In this context, it
is a classification problem rather than an image generation task, so
pixel-wise error minimization is not directly applicable. However, the
choice of loss function and optimization strategy can still impact the
model's ability to generalize and perform well on unseen data, as
discussed in the previous points. The real mathematics in the
categorical cross-entropy loss function is based on the concept of
maximum likelihood estimation, where the model is trained to maximize
the likelihood of the observed data given the model parameters. This
involves minimizing the negative log-likelihood, which is equivalent to
minimizing the categorical cross-entropy loss in multi-class
classification problems. The optimization process involves adjusting the
model parameters to minimize this loss function, which can be achieved
using various optimization algorithms such as SGD, Adam, or RMSProp, as
discussed in this module. The categorical cross entropy measures the
dissimilarity between the predicted probability distribution and the
true distribution (one-hot encoded labels) for multi-class
classification problems. It is defined as:
\[L = -\sum_{i=1}^{N} \sum_{j=1}^{C} y_{ij} \log(p_{ij})\]

Where \(N\) is the number of samples, \(C\) is the number of classes,
\(y_{ij}\) is the true label (1 if sample \(i\) belongs to class \(j\),
0 otherwise), and \(p_{ij}\) is the predicted probability that sample
\(i\) belongs to class \(j\). The optimization process involves
minimizing this loss function with respect to the model parameters,
which can be achieved using various optimization algorithms such as SGD,
Adam, or RMSProp, as discussed in this module. The choice of
optimization algorithm and hyperparameters can significantly impact the
convergence and performance of the model on the given dataset. This
optimization uses the properties of the loss function, such as its
smoothness and convexity, to guide the search for optimal parameters.
The optimization process iteratively updates the model parameters in the
direction that minimizes the loss function, ultimately leading to a
model that can effectively classify the iris dataset with high accuracy
and generalization performance.

\section{Advanced Networks for image
processing}\label{advanced-networks-for-image-processing}

Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) are a class of deep learning models
that have shown remarkable performance in image-related tasks. They are
designed to automatically and adaptively learn spatial hierarchies of
features from input images, making them particularly effective for tasks
such as image classification, object detection, and image generation. A
simple example of CNN for classifying MNIST Fashion dataset can be
implemented as follows:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# Step 1: Import necessary libraries}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ numpy }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ np}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.pyplot }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ plt}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ tensorflow }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ tf}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ tensorflow }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ keras}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ tensorflow.keras }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ layers}
\ImportTok{from}\NormalTok{ tensorflow.keras.models }\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ Model}
\ImportTok{import}\NormalTok{ matplotlib.gridspec }\ImportTok{as}\NormalTok{ gridspec}

\CommentTok{\# Step 2: Load and preprocess Fashion{-}MNIST dataset}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Loading Fashion{-}MNIST dataset..."}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{fashion\_mnist }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ keras.datasets.fashion\_mnist}
\NormalTok{(train\_images, train\_labels), (test\_images, test\_labels) }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ fashion\_mnist.load\_data()}

\CommentTok{\# Class names for Fashion{-}MNIST}
\NormalTok{class\_names }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}T{-}shirt/top\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Trouser\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Pullover\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Dress\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Coat\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
               \StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Sandal\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Shirt\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Sneaker\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Bag\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Ankle boot\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}

\CommentTok{\# Normalize pixel values to [0, 1]}
\NormalTok{train\_images }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ train\_images.astype(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}float32\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{255.0}
\NormalTok{test\_images }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_images.astype(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}float32\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{255.0}

\CommentTok{\# Reshape images to add channel dimension (28, 28, 1)}
\NormalTok{train\_images }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ train\_images.reshape(train\_images.shape[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{test\_images }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_images.reshape(test\_images.shape[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Training data shape: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{train\_images}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{shape}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Test data shape: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{test\_images}\SpecialCharTok{.}\NormalTok{shape}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 3: Display sample images from the dataset}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ display\_sample\_images(images, labels, num\_rows}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, num\_cols}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{    plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(num\_rows }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ num\_cols):}
\NormalTok{        plt.subplot(num\_rows, num\_cols, i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.imshow(images[i].reshape(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{), cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.title(class\_names[labels[i]])}
\NormalTok{        plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Sample Fashion{-}MNIST Images\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{1.02}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\NormalTok{display\_sample\_images(train\_images, train\_labels)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 4: Build the CNN model using Functional API instead of Sequential}
\CommentTok{\# This approach makes it easier to extract intermediate layer outputs}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ create\_cnn\_functional\_model():}
    \CommentTok{\# Define input layer}
\NormalTok{    inputs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ keras.Input(shape}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{), name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}input\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# First Convolutional Block}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Conv2D(}\DecValTok{32}\NormalTok{, (}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{), activation}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, padding}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}same\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(inputs)}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.MaxPooling2D((}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}pool1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Second Convolutional Block}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Conv2D(}\DecValTok{64}\NormalTok{, (}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{), activation}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, padding}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}same\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.MaxPooling2D((}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}pool2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Third Convolutional Block}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Conv2D(}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{, (}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{), activation}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, padding}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}same\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.MaxPooling2D((}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}pool3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Flatten and Dense layers}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Flatten(name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}flatten\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Dense(}\DecValTok{128}\NormalTok{, activation}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}relu\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dense1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
\NormalTok{    x }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Dropout(}\FloatTok{0.5}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}dropout\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
\NormalTok{    outputs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layers.Dense(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{, activation}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}softmax\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}output\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)(x)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Create model}
\NormalTok{    model }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ keras.Model(inputs}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{inputs, outputs}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{outputs, name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}fashion\_cnn\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ model}

\CommentTok{\# Create the model}
\NormalTok{model }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ create\_cnn\_functional\_model()}
\NormalTok{model.summary()}

\CommentTok{\# Step 5: Compile and train the model}
\NormalTok{model.}\BuiltInTok{compile}\NormalTok{(optimizer}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}adam\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{              loss}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}sparse\_categorical\_crossentropy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{              metrics}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{])}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{Training the model..."}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{history }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ model.fit(train\_images, train\_labels, }
\NormalTok{                   epochs}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{,  }\CommentTok{\# Reduced epochs for faster execution}
\NormalTok{                   batch\_size}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{64}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                   validation\_split}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.2}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{                   verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 6: Evaluate the model}
\NormalTok{test\_loss, test\_accuracy }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ model.evaluate(test\_images, test\_labels, verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Test accuracy: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{test\_accuracy}\SpecialCharTok{:.4f\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 7: Create feature extraction model}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ create\_feature\_extractor(model):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Create a model that extracts features from intermediate layers}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \CommentTok{\# Define the layers we want to extract}
\NormalTok{    layer\_names }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    layer\_outputs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [model.get\_layer(name).output }\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ name }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ layer\_names]}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Create feature extractor model}
\NormalTok{    feature\_extractor }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ Model(inputs}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{model.}\BuiltInTok{input}\NormalTok{, outputs}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{layer\_outputs)}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ feature\_extractor}

\CommentTok{\# Create feature extractor}
\NormalTok{feature\_extractor }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ create\_feature\_extractor(model)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 8: Visualize feature maps for a single image}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ visualize\_feature\_maps(image, label, feature\_extractor, layer\_names}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Visualize feature maps from different layers}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \CommentTok{\# Get feature maps}
\NormalTok{    feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ feature\_extractor.predict(image[np.newaxis, ...], verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# If only one layer output, wrap in list}
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not} \BuiltInTok{isinstance}\NormalTok{(feature\_maps, }\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [feature\_maps]}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Create figure}
\NormalTok{    fig }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{15}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Plot original image}
\NormalTok{    plt.subplot(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.imshow(image.reshape(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{), cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}Original: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[label]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Plot feature maps for each layer}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ layer\_idx, (layer\_name, features) }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{zip}\NormalTok{(layer\_names, feature\_maps)):}
        \CommentTok{\# Show 5 feature maps from this layer}
\NormalTok{        num\_features }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, features.shape[}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(num\_features):}
\NormalTok{            plot\_idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layer\_idx }\OperatorTok{*} \DecValTok{5} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2}
            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ plot\_idx }\OperatorTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{:  }\CommentTok{\# Ensure we don\textquotesingle{}t exceed subplot grid}
\NormalTok{                plt.subplot(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, plot\_idx)}
\NormalTok{                plt.imshow(features[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, :, :, i], cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}viridis\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{                plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
                \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ i }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{                    plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{layer\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Hide empty subplots}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(layer\_names)}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{5} \OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{17}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        plt.subplot(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, i)}
\NormalTok{        plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Feature Maps: From Edges to High{-}Level Features\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.98}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\CommentTok{\# Step 9: Demonstrate feature hierarchy}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ demonstrate\_feature\_hierarchy(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels, num\_samples}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Demonstrate feature hierarchy for multiple sample images}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"FEATURE HIERARCHY DEMONSTRATION"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Select random samples}
\NormalTok{    indices }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.random.choice(}\BuiltInTok{len}\NormalTok{(test\_images), num\_samples, replace}\OperatorTok{=}\VariableTok{False}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ idx }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ indices:}
\NormalTok{        image }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_images[idx]}
\NormalTok{        label }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_labels[idx]}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Get prediction from original model}
\NormalTok{        prediction }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ model.predict(image[np.newaxis, ...], verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        pred\_class }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.argmax(prediction)}
\NormalTok{        confidence }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.}\BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{(prediction)}
        
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Image }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{idx}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{: True class = }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[label]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{"}\NormalTok{)}
        \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"Predicted: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[pred\_class]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ (confidence: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{confidence}\SpecialCharTok{:.3f\}}\SpecialStringTok{)"}\NormalTok{)}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Visualize feature maps}
\NormalTok{        visualize\_feature\_maps(image, label, feature\_extractor)}

\CommentTok{\# Run the demonstration}
\NormalTok{demonstrate\_feature\_hierarchy(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels, num\_samples}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 10: Visualize learned filters}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ visualize\_learned\_filters(model, layer\_name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Visualize the learned filters from a convolutional layer}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    layer }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ model.get\_layer(layer\_name)}
\NormalTok{    weights }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ layer.get\_weights()[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]  }\CommentTok{\# Get the kernels}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Normalize weights for better visualization}
\NormalTok{    weights }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (weights }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ weights.}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{()) }\OperatorTok{/}\NormalTok{ (weights.}\BuiltInTok{max}\NormalTok{() }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ weights.}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{() }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{1e{-}8}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    num\_filters }\OperatorTok{=} \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, weights.shape[}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])}
\NormalTok{    num\_rows }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (num\_filters }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{//} \DecValTok{4}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.figure(figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{3}\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{num\_rows))}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(num\_filters):}
\NormalTok{        plt.subplot(num\_rows, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.imshow(weights[:, :, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, i], cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}viridis\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}Filter }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}Learned Filters from }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{layer\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{ Layer\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.98}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"VISUALIZING LEARNED FILTERS"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{visualize\_learned\_filters(model, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 11: Show feature map statistics}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ analyze\_feature\_statistics(feature\_extractor, sample\_image, sample\_label):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Analyze and compare feature maps across layers}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    layer\_names }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{    feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ feature\_extractor.predict(sample\_image[np.newaxis, ...], verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not} \BuiltInTok{isinstance}\NormalTok{(feature\_maps, }\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [feature\_maps]}
    
\NormalTok{    fig, axes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Original image}
\NormalTok{    axes[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].imshow(sample\_image.reshape(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{), cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    axes[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}Input: }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[sample\_label]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    axes[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Statistics for each layer}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ i, (layer\_name, features) }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{zip}\NormalTok{(layer\_names, feature\_maps)):}
        \CommentTok{\# Calculate mean activation across all feature maps}
\NormalTok{        mean\_activation }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.mean(features[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], axis}\OperatorTok{={-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
        
\NormalTok{        im }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ axes[i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].imshow(mean\_activation, cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}hot\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{layer\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Mean Activation\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        plt.colorbar(im, ax}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{axes[i}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Feature Map Statistics Across Layers\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\CommentTok{\# Demonstrate with a sample}
\NormalTok{sample\_idx }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{10}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialStringTok{f"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Feature statistics for }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[test\_labels[sample\_idx]]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{:"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{analyze\_feature\_statistics(feature\_extractor, test\_images[sample\_idx], test\_labels[sample\_idx])}

\CommentTok{\# Step 12: Training history visualization}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ plot\_training\_history(history):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Plot training and validation accuracy/loss}
\CommentTok{    """}
\NormalTok{    fig, (ax1, ax2) }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Accuracy}
\NormalTok{    ax1.plot(history.history[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{], label}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Training Accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}o\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.plot(history.history[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}val\_accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{], label}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Validation Accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}o\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.set\_title(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Model Accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.set\_xlabel(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Epoch\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.set\_ylabel(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Accuracy\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax1.legend()}
\NormalTok{    ax1.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Loss}
\NormalTok{    ax2.plot(history.history[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{], label}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Training Loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}o\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.plot(history.history[}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}val\_loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{], label}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Validation Loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, marker}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}o\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.set\_title(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Model Loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.set\_xlabel(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Epoch\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.set\_ylabel(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Loss\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ax2.legend()}
\NormalTok{    ax2.grid(}\VariableTok{True}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\NormalTok{plot\_training\_history(history)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 13: Comprehensive feature hierarchy explanation}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ explain\_feature\_hierarchy\_comprehensive(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Provide a comprehensive explanation of feature hierarchy with examples}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"UNDERSTANDING CNN FEATURE HIERARCHY"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    explanation }\OperatorTok{=} \StringTok{"""}
\StringTok{    HOW CNNs LEARN HIERARCHICAL FEATURES:}
\StringTok{    }
\StringTok{    🔵 LAYER 1 (conv1) {-} LOW{-}LEVEL FEATURES:}
\StringTok{       • Detects simple patterns like edges (horizontal, vertical, diagonal)}
\StringTok{       • Identifies corners and basic textures}
\StringTok{       • These are universal feature detectors (similar across all images)}
\StringTok{       • Feature maps show edge{-}like activations}
\StringTok{       • Output size: 14x14 with 32 channels}
\StringTok{    }
\StringTok{    🟢 LAYER 2 (conv2) {-} MID{-}LEVEL FEATURES:}
\StringTok{       • Combines edges to form shapes (circles, rectangles, patterns)}
\StringTok{       • Learns textures and repeating patterns}
\StringTok{       • Starts recognizing object parts (sleeves, collars, straps)}
\StringTok{       • Feature maps show pattern{-}like activations}
\StringTok{       • Output size: 7x7 with 64 channels}
\StringTok{    }
\StringTok{    🔴 LAYER 3 (conv3) {-} HIGH{-}LEVEL FEATURES:}
\StringTok{       • Learns complex, task{-}specific features}
\StringTok{       • Detects complete object parts and their arrangements}
\StringTok{       • Features become more abstract and semantically meaningful}
\StringTok{       • Feature maps show object{-}part activations}
\StringTok{       • Output size: 3x3 with 128 channels}
\StringTok{    }
\StringTok{    ⚪ DENSE LAYERS {-} CLASSIFICATION:}
\StringTok{       • Combines high{-}level features}
\StringTok{       • Makes final classification decisions}
\StringTok{       • Learns which feature combinations indicate specific clothing items}
\StringTok{    """}
    
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(explanation)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Show examples of feature progression}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"{-}"}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"EXAMPLE: Feature progression for different clothing items"}\NormalTok{)}
    \BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"{-}"}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{60}\NormalTok{)}
    
    \CommentTok{\# Select examples from different classes}
\NormalTok{    example\_indices }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{9}\NormalTok{]  }\CommentTok{\# T{-}shirt, Pullover, Sandal, Ankle boot}
    
\NormalTok{    fig, axes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ row, idx }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(example\_indices):}
\NormalTok{        image }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_images[idx]}
\NormalTok{        label }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ test\_labels[idx]}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Get feature maps}
\NormalTok{        feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ feature\_extractor.predict(image[np.newaxis, ...], verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not} \BuiltInTok{isinstance}\NormalTok{(feature\_maps, }\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{            feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [feature\_maps]}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Original image}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].imshow(image.reshape(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{), cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[label]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Original\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Feature maps from each layer}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ col, (layer\_name, features) }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{zip}\NormalTok{([}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv1\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv2\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, }\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}conv3\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{], feature\_maps)):}
            \CommentTok{\# Show first 4 feature maps as examples}
            \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ f }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, features.shape[}\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{])):}
\NormalTok{                col\_idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ col}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{+}\NormalTok{ f }\OperatorTok{+} \DecValTok{1}
                \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ col\_idx }\OperatorTok{\textless{}} \DecValTok{5}\NormalTok{:  }\CommentTok{\# Ensure we don\textquotesingle{}t exceed subplot grid}
\NormalTok{                  axes[row, col\_idx].imshow(features[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, :, :, f], cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}viridis\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{                  axes[row, col\_idx].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{layer\_name}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{F}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{f}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{                  axes[row, col\_idx].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Feature Progression Across Layers for Different Clothing Items\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.98}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\NormalTok{explain\_feature\_hierarchy\_comprehensive(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 14: Visualize activation patterns}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ visualize\_activation\_patterns(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels):}
    \CommentTok{"""}
\CommentTok{    Visualize how different classes activate different feature maps}
\CommentTok{    """}
    \CommentTok{\# Select one image from each class}
\NormalTok{    class\_examples }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ []}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ class\_id }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{        idx }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.where(test\_labels }\OperatorTok{==}\NormalTok{ class\_id)[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{][}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{        class\_examples.append((test\_images[idx], class\_id))}
    
\NormalTok{    fig, axes }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ plt.subplots(}\DecValTok{10}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{, figsize}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{(}\DecValTok{16}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{))}
    
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ row, (image, label) }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(class\_examples):}
        \CommentTok{\# Get feature maps}
\NormalTok{        feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ feature\_extractor.predict(image[np.newaxis, ...], verbose}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{)}
        \ControlFlowTok{if} \KeywordTok{not} \BuiltInTok{isinstance}\NormalTok{(feature\_maps, }\BuiltInTok{list}\NormalTok{):}
\NormalTok{            feature\_maps }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ [feature\_maps]}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Original image}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].imshow(image.reshape(}\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{28}\NormalTok{), cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}gray\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}}\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{class\_names[label]}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\SpecialStringTok{\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{        axes[row, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
        
        \CommentTok{\# Show mean activation for each layer}
        \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ col, features }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{enumerate}\NormalTok{(feature\_maps):}
\NormalTok{            mean\_activation }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.mean(features[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], axis}\OperatorTok{={-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            axes[row, col}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].imshow(mean\_activation, cmap}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}hot\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
            \ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ row }\OperatorTok{==} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}
\NormalTok{                axes[row, col}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].set\_title(}\SpecialStringTok{f\textquotesingle{}Layer }\SpecialCharTok{\{}\NormalTok{col}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\SpecialCharTok{\}}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\SpecialStringTok{Mean Activation\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{8}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{            axes[row, col}\OperatorTok{+}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{].axis(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}off\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
    
\NormalTok{    plt.suptitle(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}Activation Patterns Across Different Classes\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{, fontsize}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{14}\NormalTok{, y}\OperatorTok{=}\FloatTok{0.98}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    plt.tight\_layout()}
\NormalTok{    plt.show()}

\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"CLASS{-}SPECIFIC ACTIVATION PATTERNS"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{visualize\_activation\_patterns(feature\_extractor, test\_images, test\_labels)}

\CommentTok{\# Step 15: Final summary}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{"} \OperatorTok{+} \StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"SUMMARY: CNN FEATURE LEARNING HIERARCHY"}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"="}\OperatorTok{*}\DecValTok{70}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"""}
\StringTok{✅ Layer 1 (conv1): Edge and Corner Detectors}
\StringTok{   • Responds to simple patterns}
\StringTok{   • Universal feature detectors}
\StringTok{   • Visualized as oriented edges}

\StringTok{✅ Layer 2 (conv2): Pattern and Shape Detectors}
\StringTok{   • Combines edges into shapes}
\StringTok{   • Learns textures and patterns}
\StringTok{   • Starts recognizing object parts}

\StringTok{✅ Layer 3 (conv3): Object Part Detectors}
\StringTok{   • Learns complex, task{-}specific features}
\StringTok{   • Responds to semantic concepts}
\StringTok{   • Abstract representations}

\StringTok{✅ Dense Layers: Classifiers}
\StringTok{   • Combines high{-}level features}
\StringTok{   • Makes final classification decisions}
\StringTok{   • Learns class{-}specific feature combinations}

\StringTok{The network progressively builds more abstract and }
\StringTok{task{-}specific representations from raw pixels to }
\StringTok{final classification decisions.}
\StringTok{"""}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{model.save(}\StringTok{\textquotesingle{}fashion\_mnist\_functional\_model.h5\textquotesingle{}}\NormalTok{)}
\BuiltInTok{print}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"}\CharTok{\textbackslash{}n}\StringTok{Model saved as \textquotesingle{}fashion\_mnist\_functional\_model.h5\textquotesingle{}"}\NormalTok{)}

\begin{verbatim}

================================================================================
UNDERSTANDING CNN FEATURE HIERARCHY
================================================================================

    HOW CNNs LEARN HIERARCHICAL FEATURES:
    
    🔵 LAYER 1 (conv1) - LOW-LEVEL FEATURES:
       • Detects simple patterns like edges (horizontal, vertical, diagonal)
       • Identifies corners and basic textures
       • These are universal feature detectors (similar across all images)
       • Feature maps show edge-like activations
       • Output size: 14x14 with 32 channels
    
    🟢 LAYER 2 (conv2) - MID-LEVEL FEATURES:
       • Combines edges to form shapes (circles, rectangles, patterns)
       • Learns textures and repeating patterns
       • Starts recognizing object parts (sleeves, collars, straps)
       • Feature maps show pattern-like activations
       • Output size: 7x7 with 64 channels
    
    🔴 LAYER 3 (conv3) - HIGH-LEVEL FEATURES:
       • Learns complex, task-specific features
       • Detects complete object parts and their arrangements
       • Features become more abstract and semantically meaningful
       • Feature maps show object-part activations
       • Output size: 3x3 with 128 channels
    
    ⚪ DENSE LAYERS - CLASSIFICATION:
       • Combines high-level features
       • Makes final classification decisions
       • Learns which feature combinations indicate specific clothing items
    

------------------------------------------------------------
EXAMPLE: Feature progression for different clothing items
------------------------------------------------------------
\end{verbatim}

\section{Capstone Project: AI-Enhanced Campus
Optimization}\label{capstone-project-ai-enhanced-campus-optimization}

\subsection{Project Foundation}\label{project-foundation}

\subsubsection{Integration of All
Modules}\label{integration-of-all-modules}

This capstone project synthesizes concepts from all five modules:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Module 1:} Linear constraints and formulations
\item
  \textbf{Module 2:} Nonlinear cost modeling
\item
  \textbf{Module 3:} Project planning and scheduling
\item
  \textbf{Module 4:} Graph algorithms and network optimization
\item
  \textbf{Module 5:} Machine learning and gradient optimization
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{Project Vision}\label{project-vision}

Develop an intelligent campus optimization system that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Predicts} facility demands using machine learning
\item
  \textbf{Optimizes} resource allocation using advanced algorithms
\item
  \textbf{Adapts} to changing conditions in real-time
\item
  \textbf{Visualizes} optimal solutions and provides insights
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{System Architecture}\label{system-architecture}

\textbf{Data Layer:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Historical campus operations data
\item
  Real-time sensor data
\item
  Environmental and contextual data
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Model Layer:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Demand prediction models
\item
  Optimization algorithms
\item
  Constraint handlers
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Application Layer:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Dashboard for campus operations
\item
  Real-time optimization engine
\item
  Reporting and analytics
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Capstone Project
Requirements}\label{capstone-project-requirements}

\subsubsection{Part A: Predictive Modeling (5
Marks)}\label{part-a-predictive-modeling-5-marks}

\textbf{Objective:} Develop machine learning models to predict campus
facility demands.

\textbf{Tasks:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Feature Engineering:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Temporal features (hour, day, month, holidays)
  \item
    Environmental features (weather, temperature)
  \item
    Event features (exams, sports, cultural events)
  \item
    Historical demand patterns
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Model Development:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Time series forecasting models
  \item
    Regression models for demand prediction
  \item
    Ensemble methods for improved accuracy
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Optimization Integration:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Use predictions as input to optimization models
  \item
    Handle prediction uncertainty in optimization
  \item
    Adaptive models that update with new data
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Part B: Integrated Optimization System (6
Marks)}\label{part-b-integrated-optimization-system-6-marks}

\textbf{Objective:} Create a comprehensive optimization system that uses
predictions to make optimal decisions.

\textbf{Tasks:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Multi-Period Optimization:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Dynamic optimization across time horizons
  \item
    Handling of time-dependent constraints
  \item
    Integration of predicted vs actual demands
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Stochastic Optimization:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Handle uncertainty in predictions
  \item
    Robust optimization approaches
  \item
    Scenario-based planning
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Real-Time Adaptation:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Update optimization based on real-time data
  \item
    Handle unexpected events and disruptions
  \item
    Provide alternative solutions
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Part C: Advanced Algorithm Implementation (5
Marks)}\label{part-c-advanced-algorithm-implementation-5-marks}

\textbf{Objective:} Implement sophisticated optimization algorithms for
complex campus problems.

\textbf{Tasks:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Gradient-Based Optimization:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Implement Adam optimizer for parameter tuning
  \item
    Learning rate scheduling and adaptation
  \item
    Handling of non-convex objective functions
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Metaheuristic Integration:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Combine gradient methods with heuristic approaches
  \item
    Hybrid algorithms for complex constraints
  \item
    Multi-objective optimization techniques
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Large-Scale Optimization:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Distributed optimization approaches
  \item
    Parallel processing for campus-scale problems
  \item
    Efficient data structures for optimization
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsubsection{Part D: System Deployment \& Evaluation (4
Marks)}\label{part-d-system-deployment-evaluation-4-marks}

\textbf{Objective:} Deploy and evaluate the complete system.

\textbf{Tasks:}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  \textbf{Performance Metrics:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Optimization solution quality
  \item
    Computational efficiency
  \item
    Prediction accuracy
  \item
    Practical feasibility
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Comparative Analysis:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Baseline vs optimized solutions
  \item
    Different algorithm comparisons
  \item
    Sensitivity to parameter choices
  \end{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Business Impact Assessment:}

  \begin{itemize}
  \tightlist
  \item
    Cost savings quantification
  \item
    Service improvement metrics
  \item
    Environmental impact reduction
  \item
    Scalability assessment
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Sample Capstone Problem}\label{sample-capstone-problem}

\textbf{Problem Statement:} Develop an AI-driven system for Campus City
that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Predicts hourly demands for all 15 facilities
\item
  Optimizes warehouse operations and delivery schedules
\item
  Adapts to real-time events (emergencies, weather)
\item
  Provides dashboard for operations management
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Solution Components:}

\textbf{Prediction Module:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  LSTM neural networks for time series prediction
\item
  Gradient-based optimization for model training
\item
  Uncertainty quantification for predictions
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Optimization Module:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Multi-period mixed-integer programming
\item
  Stochastic optimization for demand uncertainty
\item
  Real-time re-optimization engine
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Integration Layer:}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  API for data exchange between modules
\item
  Visualization dashboard
\item
  Alert system for exceptional situations
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Capstone Design Questions}\label{capstone-design-questions}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\item
  How would you design a neural network architecture for predicting
  campus facility demands that captures both temporal patterns and
  external factors?
\item
  What gradient optimization method would you choose for training your
  prediction models and why?
\item
  How would you handle the trade-off between prediction model complexity
  and optimization solution time in a real-time system?
\item
  Design a multi-objective optimization approach that balances cost
  minimization with service level maximization for campus operations.
\item
  How would you validate that your AI-enhanced optimization system
  actually improves campus operations compared to traditional methods?
\end{enumerate}

\section{Review Questions}\label{review-questions-20}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Describe the concept of regularization in the machine learning
  context. Given any three commonly used regularization in regression
  models.
\item
  Explain the concept of momentum in loss optimization, providing a
  clear rationale. Also, write the algorithm for the momentum method.
\item
  Explain the bias-variance trade-off in machine learning. How does it
  relate to model complexity and generalization?
\item
  What is the purpose of a validation set in machine learning, and how
  does it differ from the training and test sets?
\item
  Describe the concept of overfitting in machine learning. What are some
  common techniques to prevent overfitting?
\item
  Explain the concept of cross-validation and how it can be used to
  evaluate the performance of a machine learning model.
\item
  Explain the gradient descent method for minimizing the loss function.
  What are the common issues with this method, and suggest a method to
  overcome them?
\item
  Minimize the quadratic function \(f(x)= x^2- 4x + 4\) using gradient
  descent with momentum starting from \(x_0= 0\), with learning rate
  \(\eta = 0.1\) and \(\gamma=0.9\) in 4 iterations.
\item
  What is the effect of the learning rate on gradient descent
  convergence? Provide examples of problems caused by too large and too
  small learning rates.
\item
  Compare RMSProp and Adam optimizers. When would you choose one over
  the other for campus demand prediction?
\item
  Explain how momentum helps gradient descent overcome oscillations in
  narrow valleys.
\item
  Minimize the quadratic function \(f(x)= x^2- 4x + 4\) using gradient
  descent starting from \(x_0= 0\), with learning rate \(\eta = 0.1\) in
  4 iterations.
\item
  Explain different types of gradient descent methods and compare them
  with any three dominant advantages and disadvantages.
\item
  Let \(y_i\) be the ground truth and \(\hat{y}_i\) be the predicted
  value of a functional approximation \(y = f(\theta_0 + \theta_1 x)\),
  where \(\theta_0, \theta_1\) are parameters. Formulate the mean square
  error for this predictive function, considering \(n\) samples. Also
  derive the update formula for both \(\theta_0\) and \(\theta_1\) using
  the gradient descent method.
\item
  Explain the concept of learning rate in gradient descent. What are the
  potential consequences of choosing a learning rate that is too high or
  too low?
\item
  Describe the Adam optimization algorithm and explain how it combines
  the advantages of both momentum and RMSProp.
\item
  Explain the concept of stochastic optimization and how it differs from
  traditional optimization methods. Provide an example of a stochastic
  optimization algorithm.
\item
  Discuss the challenges of integrating AI techniques with traditional
  optimization methods. How can these challenges be addressed in
  practice?
\end{enumerate}

\section{Module 5 Summary}\label{module-5-summary}

\subsection{Key Concepts Mastered}\label{key-concepts-mastered-2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  ✅ Gradient descent fundamentals and mathematical foundations
\item
  ✅ Advanced gradient methods (Momentum, RMSProp, Adam)
\item
  ✅ Optimization in machine learning pipelines
\item
  ✅ Stochastic optimization methods and applications
\item
